What happened: Oracle ( ORCL ) and CoreWeave ( CRWV ) came out in support of OpenAI on Tuesday after the Wall Street Journal reported that the AI developer recently missed sales and user targets, renewing concerns about overspending in the sector. Stock market reaction: Shares of companies partnering with OpenAI sank after the report signaled that OpenAI\u2019s business was slowing. Software giant Oracle and data center provider CoreWeave closed down roughly 4% and 5%, respectively. Oracle defended the AI developer on X, writing, \u201cWe\u2019re incredibly excited about our partnership with OpenAI and remain focused on building and delivering the capacity they need to support rapidly growing demand.\u201d Advertisement Advertisement Advertisement Advertisement CoreWeave struck a similar tone in a statement to Yahoo Finance, but noted it had other partners. \u201cOpenAI is a terrific partner, but not our only one,\u201d a CoreWeave spokesperson said, adding that the company has an \u201cexpanding set of customers like Meta Platforms, Anthropic, Microsoft, Google, IBM, Perplexity AI, Jane Street, and many others.\u201d Shares of other companies that have partnerships with OpenAI also fell on Tuesday, including chip giants AMD ( AMD ) and Nvidia ( NVDA ). Citing people familiar with the matter, the WSJ report said the startup fell short of its internal goal of 1 billion weekly active users for OpenAI\u2019s ChatGPT by year-end. It also reportedly missed its annual revenue target for the product. Meanwhile, Google\u2019s ( GOOGL , GOOG ) competing AI bot, Gemini, grew over the past year, eating into OpenAI\u2019s market share. What else you need to know: OpenAI has been on a spending spree as CEO Sam Altman has raced to secure computing power. The missed metrics have renewed concerns about the company\u2019s mounting costs amid increasing competition. The report"

    "content": "Galaxy Digital first-quarter loss narrows, AI push grows Search / News Video Prices Research Consensus 2026 Data & Indices Sponsored Search / en Finance Share Share this article Copy link X icon X (Twitter) LinkedIn Facebook Email Galaxy Digital delivers first data center tranche to CoreWeave after narrowing Q1 loss Galaxy secured approval to double its Helios data center power capacity to over 1.6GW, building on its strategic expansion into AI infrastructure. By Francisco Rodrigues , AI Boost | Edited by Sheldon Reback Apr 28, 2026, 2:09 p.m. 1 min read Make preferred on (Taylor Vick/Unsplash) What to know : Galaxy Digital's first-quarter net loss narrowed to $216 million as it looked to steadier revenue-generating businesses. After the quarter, the company handed its first data-center hall to CoreWeave. It also secured approval to double the Helios data center power capacity to over 1.6GW, signifying a major strategic expansion into AI infrastructure. Galaxy Digital (GLXY) narrowed its first-quarter loss as a shift in business mix and tighter financial management outweighed a decline in cryptocurrency prices. The company lost $216 million, or 49 cents a share, less than the 59 cents estimated by analysts. Revenue dropped to $10.2 billion from $12.9 billion in the year-earlier quarter. The company is increasingly focusing on the growing demand for data centers, and this month delivered its first data hall at the Helios campus in Texas to CoreWeave (CRWV), marking the start of revenue under a long-term lease tied to artificial intelligence workloads. \"Adjusted gross profit remained broadly stable, reflecting a shift in the business mix as recurring fee revenue and transaction income continue to scale and provide greater resilience in softer market conditions,\" the company said in a statement. \"Disciplined expense management during the quarter helped narrow the adjusted EBITDA loss, underscoring a focus on operating efficiency in more challenging environments.\" The Helios facility is set to deliver 133 megawatts of computing power by the end of the second quarter. The company also secured approval for an additional 830 megawatts of power at the site, bringing total capacity to more than 1.6 gigawatts. GLXY shares fell for a second day, and were recently 0.84% lower at $24.84. CRWV closed Tuesday at approximately $105.53, having traded between $102.50 and $110.00 during the session on volume of around 20.86 million shares, below its daily average of 30.41 million. Over the past 52 weeks the stock has moved between a low of $39.50 and a high of $187.00, making its current level a meaningful retreat from peak euphoria but a dramatic recovery from its post-IPO lows. NFLX closed the same day at $92.27, trading between $90.02 and $92.34 on volume of approximately 33.42 million shares. Over the past 52 weeks Netflix has ranged from a low of $75.01 to a high of $134.12, meaning it was sitting more than 31% below its 52-week peak heading into this week\u2019s sessions. The reasons behind each pullback are completely different, and so are the underlying business trajectories. CoreWeave\u2019s retreat from $187 is a valuation story wrapped in a deal-flow story. The company has been one of the most active dealmakers in the AI infrastructure space over recent weeks, securing a $21 billion expansion of its partnership with Meta Platforms (NASDAQ: META), a multi-year agreement to power Anthropic\u2019s Claude models, and a $6 billion committed services deal with Jane Street. It also raised $3.5 billion in convertible notes to fund continued expansion. Its EBITDA margin sits at 47.5% and the business is clearly generating real cash from the AI compute buildout. Yet the stock trades at a negative P/E ratio because it is still loss-making at the net income level, with last quarter\u2019s EPS coming in at negative $0.56 against an estimate of negative $0.50. The market is pricing in a growth story that requires continued flawless execution, and the 52-week high of $187 reflected a moment of peak optimism that has since been tested by concerns about financing costs, insider selling, and the broader question of whether neocloud economics can remain this attractive as hyperscalers build more capacity of their own. Netflix\u2019s situation is more about perception than fundamentals. Q1 2026 earnings actually beat expectations, with EPS of $1.23 against estimates of $0.79 and revenue of $12.25 billion. Free cash flow exploded to $5.2 billion in the quarter. Full-year guidance for revenue of $50.7 billion to $51.7 billion was left unchanged. The company also announced a fresh $25 billion share buyback programme. Two things spooked the market. The guidance for Q2 came in softer than some investors had hoped, and co-founder Reed Hastings announced he"

    "content": "Nebius Weighs AI21 Labs Deal As CoreWeave Capacity Tests Growth Story - Simply Wall St News Home Portfolios Watchlist Community Discover Screener Sign Up or Login Home Portfolios Watchlist Community Discover Screener Advertisement United States / Software / NasdaqGS:NBIS Nebius Weighs AI21 Labs Deal As CoreWeave Capacity Tests Growth Story April 28, 2026 Simply Wall St Reviewed by Bailey Pemberton Nebius Group (NasdaqGS:NBIS) is in active talks to acquire Israeli AI firm AI21 Labs after Nvidia exited earlier negotiations. The company is also expanding its AI infrastructure footprint through new data center capacity contracts with cloud specialist CoreWeave. These developments come as Nebius shares trade at $144.96, following a 61.2% return year to date and a very large 1 year gain. Nebius Group, ticker NasdaqGS:NBIS, is pursuing AI21 Labs at a time when its own share price sits at $144.96 and recent returns have been strong. The stock is up 61.2% year to date and around 7x over the past 1 year, which puts extra attention on how new AI assets and infrastructure deals might shape the company\u2019s next phase. For investors, the AI21 Labs talks and new CoreWeave contracts highlight how Nebius is positioning its AI software stack alongside capacity in key data center markets. The outcome of the acquisition negotiations, together with execution on these infrastructure agreements, may influence the company\u2019s future product mix, customer reach, and how the durability of recent share price gains is perceived. Stay updated on the most important news stories for Nebius Group by adding it to your watchlist or portfolio . Alternatively, explore our Community to discover new perspectives on Nebius Group. NasdaqGS:NBIS Earnings & Revenue Growth as at Apr 2026 2 things going right for Nebius Group that this headline doesn't cover. This potential acquisition of AI21 Labs sits alongside growing infrastructure commitments and helps fill out Nebius Group\u2019s AI stack. AI21 Labs focuses on large language models and enterprise AI tools, which could give Nebius more of its own higher level software to run on the GPU capacity it is already lining up with partners such as CoreWeave and TD SYNNEX. That matters if Nebius wants to capture not just spending on raw compute, but also spending on applied AI use cases where customers compare it with Amazon Web Services, Microsoft Azure, and Google Cloud. At the same time, more data center build outs and a sizeable software acquisition would add to capital needs and execution complexity, which investors are already watching given analyst comments on funding, earnings quality, and valuation checks. The key question is whether combining AI21 Labs, new CoreWeave contracts, and existing deployments like TD SYNNEX\u2019s reserved NVIDIA clusters on Nebius AI Cloud can support durable usage and revenue without stretching the balance sheet or increasing operational risk too far. In this article CRWV Follow your favorite stocks CREATE FREE ACCOUNT watch now VIDEO 3:17 03:17 CoreWeave CEO Intrator on company's debt load: 'Scaling is expensive' Squawk on the Street CoreWeave announced Friday that it has entered a multi-year agreement with Anthropic to power its Claude artificial intelligence models as demand for infrastructure continues to climb. Shares of the neocloud climbed 11% following the news. \"With the addition of Anthropic, nine of the leading ten AI model providers now leverage CoreWeave's platform, reflecting the growing demand for infrastructure that can support AI at scale,\" CoreWeave said in the announcement. The deal comes one day after Meta committed to spending an additional $21 billion with the AI cloud provider. The deal adds to a prior $14.2 billion commitment announced in September. CoreWeave declined to disclose the value of the Anthropic deal. The cloud provider told CNBC that of the top ten foundational AI models, Elon Musk 's xAI is the one company they do not yet work with.     "published": "2026-04-09T07:00:00Z",
    "content": "CoreWeave Takes As Much Financial Engineering As It Does Datacenter Design Jump to main content Search More topics Financial results IT spending Infrastructure Anthropic AMD Nvidia Google Microsoft Meta Platforms AWS OpenAI Database All the latest news, from all sections Search Compute Store Connect Control Code HPC AI Hyperscale Cloud Edge Enterprise NEXTPLATFORM AD CLOUD CoreWeave Takes As Much Financial Engineering As It Does Datacenter Design Timothy Prickett Morgan Timothy Prickett Morgan Co-Editor, Co-Founder, The Next Platform Published thu 9 Apr 2026 // 21:57 UTC Meta Platforms has aspirations to be one of the big AI model builders and to push the state of the art to \u201csuperintelligence,\u201d under the impression that it can sell access to personal AI agents to its more than 1 billion users worldwide. It is a reasonable business model \u2013 and a lot more sensible than the metaverse that was the obsession of company founder Mark Zuckerberg a few years and a few tens of billions of dollars ago. While the social network and AI model maker has a fondness for its own server, storage, switch, and datacenter designs, even after the launch of the Open Compute Project and its open strategy firmly charted, the company has not been shy about renting datacenter capacity when and where it needed to. And in the GenAI boom, Meta Platforms has not shied away from renting compute capacity from others. NEXTPLATFORM AD Back in September 2025, Meta Platforms inked a supplier deal with king of the neoclouds, CoreWeave, that saw the former buy $14.2 billion in AI processing capacity through December 2031 . This was a big deal for CoreWeave, which had just gone public and which needed to show that it has customers other than Microsoft renting its GPU hours. With the deal announced today in an 8-K filing with the US Securities and Exchange Commission , CoreWeave said that Meta Platforms has signed up for an additional $21 billion in AI processing capacity running out through December 2032. No one knows the ramp of that capacity usage, and it is no doubt nailed down pretty hard because the one thing CoreWeave can\u2019t have is a GPU burning capital and electricity and a customer not paying for the privilege of using it. It is not safe to assume that all $21 billion of spending happens in 2032, the incremental year, but for all we know that is exactly what will happen. This deal will bring CoreWeave\u2019s revenue backlog up to $87.8 billion, and Meta Platforms will represent 40.1 percent of that backlog. Back in March 2025 , OpenAI inked a five year deal with CoreWeave for $11.9 billion in capacity, and another $10.5 billion in capacity was added last year, bringing the total to $22.4 billion in capacity. Assuming that none of that OpenAI capacity has been activated as yet, that would represent 25.5 percent of that extended backlog. So these two customers \u2013 Meta Platforms and OpenAI \u2013 represent nearly two-thirds of the guaranteed revenue on"

A report said OpenAI has not hit some of its own growth and sales goals, and that was enough to hit the whole AI infrastructure trade on Tuesday. The damage was not small. Oracle dropped 4%, even with its $300 billion five-year compute partnership with OpenAI still in place. Broadcom lost 4%. AMD fell 3%. Nvidia slipped more than 1%. Qualcomm (QCOM) went down 0.2%, though it finished above its weakest level after getting some help Monday from reports that it is working with OpenAI on smartphone chips. Coreweave, the debt-heavy neocloud stock tied closely to AI compute demand, fell more than 5%. SoftBank, one of OpenAI\u2019s largest investors, sank about 10% in Asia. OpenAI misses growth targets and investors sell the companies tied to its compute demand The report said OpenAI has recently missed its own targets for user growth and revenue. That matters because OpenAI has signed massive deals for data centers and long-term computing power. OpeAI\u2019s finance chief Sarah Friar warned colleagues that slower sales could make it harder for OpenAI to fund future compute deals, which landed hard because OpenAI has become one of the biggest demand engines for the AI supply chain. OpenAI fought back against the criticisms though. Sam Altman and Sarah said, \u201cWe are totally aligned on buying as much compute as we can and working hard on it together every day.\u201d They also said any claim that they are split or stepping back from buying computing resources is \u201cridiculous.\u201d Oracle also stood by the partnership. A company spokesperson said, \u201cWe\u2019re incredibly excited about our partnership with OpenAI and remain focused on building and delivering the capacity they need to support rapidly growing demand.\u201d The spokesperson added, \u201cOpenAI\u2019s new 5.5 model is a significant step forward, and we expect continued momentum as access to their technology expands across cloud providers.\u201d For years, Sam has tried to secure as much data-center capacity as OpenAI can get. His view has been that not having enough computing power is the biggest limit on OpenAI\u2019s growth. That thinking led to a huge run of deals last year and"

The Best Growth Stocks to Buy Right Now Motley Fool - Wed Apr 29, 3:30PM CDT Key Points CoreWeave and Nebius both provide cloud-based AI infrastructure services. The market\u2019s demand for those services is outstripping their near-term capacity. 10 stocks we like better than CoreWeave \u203a It might seem like a risky time to buy growth stocks. The Middle East conflict remains unresolved, inflation remains high, and the Fed is reluctant to cut interest rates again. All of those challenges will likely drive investors toward more conservative investments. However, long-term investors who can look past those near-term challenges can still find some attractive growth plays in this volatile market. Let's take a closer look at two of those compelling stocks: CoreWeave (NASDAQ: CRWV) and Nebius Group (NASDAQ: NBIS) . Will AI create the world's first trillionaire? Our team just released a report on the one little-known company, called an \"Indispensable Monopoly\" providing the critical technology Nvidia and Intel both need. Continue \u00bb Image source: Getty Images. CoreWeave CoreWeave was originally an Ethereum mining company. But after the cryptocurrency market's crash in 2018, it abandoned that business model and repurposed its mining GPUs to remotely process AI tasks . CoreWeave subsequently installed more than 250,000 of Nvidia 's (NASDAQ: NVDA) top-tier data center GPUs in its servers, and it expanded its footprint from just three data centers at the end of 2022 to 43 locations in early 2026. That scale enables it to process AI tasks roughly 35 times faster and 80% cheaper than larger and more diversified cloud infrastructure platforms like Amazon Web Services (AWS) and Microsoft (NASDAQ: MSFT) Azure. From 2022 to 2025, CoreWeave's revenue surged from $16 million to $5.1 billion. Analysts expect its revenue to surge more than sevenfold to $36.7 million in 2028. That's an impressive growth trajectory for a stock that trades at less than five times this year's sales. CoreWeave's stock looks cheaper than other AI stocks because it's still deeply unprofitable and ended 2025 with a high debt-to-equity ratio of 13.8. That red ink and high leverage could force it to issue more shares to fund its capital-intensive expansion. Some investors might also be concerned about its dependence on Microsoft , which historically accounts for over half of its"

News - Stock Analysis Is CoreWeave, Inc. (CRWV) A Good Stock To Buy Now? Published on April 26, 2026 at 4:20 pm by Ricardo Pillai in News , Stock Analysis Share Is CRWV a good stock to buy? We came across a bullish thesis on CoreWeave, Inc. on Beyond The Hype \u2013 Looking Past Management & Wall Street Hype\u2019s Substack by Beyond The Hype and Shilpa Reddy. In this article, we will summarize the bulls\u2019 thesis on CRWV. CoreWeave, Inc.\u2019s share was trading at $117.43 as of April 20th. CoreWeave, Inc. operates as a cloud infrastructure technology company in the United States. CRWV delivered a strong Q4 2025, reporting revenue of $1.572 billion, up 110% year-over-year, and full-year revenue of $5.13 billion, while its backlog surged to $66.8 billion, offering significant visibility into future cash flows. Despite impressive top-line growth, aggressive deployment of infrastructure pressured margins, with Q4 adjusted operating income at $88 million, a 6% margin, and CapEx of $8.2 billion. Read More: 15 AI Stocks That Are Quietly Making Investors Rich Read More: Undervalued AI Stock Poised For Massive Gains: 10000% Upside Potential Management expects Q1 2026 to be the trough for margins before sequential recovery begins in Q2, returning to low double-digit margins by Q4. The company benefits from insatiable demand across all customer verticals, with longer weighted contract durations of around five years enhancing long-term ROI and mitigating depreciation risk, while stable and rising GPU pricing supports sustained revenue potential. CoreWeave\u2019s storage business, contributing over $100 million ARR, has increased customer stickiness and bundling leverage. FY26 CapEx, more than double FY25\u2019s, is tied to signed contracts, with active power capacity set to more than double to 1.7 GW by year-end, reflecting aggressive expansion to meet demand. Strategic partnerships, including a $2 billion investment from Nvidia, provide priority access to next-generation GPUs, although this also locks CoreWeave into a premium cost structure. Risks include margin compression from asset depreciation, reliance on debt financing, execution complexity in scaling capacity, and exposure to potential shifts in GPU dominance as AMD and ASICs gain share. While the business model remains capital-intensive and structurally challenged, strong demand, pricing power, and long-term contract visibility offer a runway to"

Why IREN\u2019s real future is in AI, according to Bernstein By Lance Datskoluo 28 April 2026 at 09:57 Share Copy link IREN is pivoting from Bitcoin mining to AI infrastructure. By 2030, analysts project $5 billion in EBITDA, with margins exceeding 80% Artificial intelligence is the real opportunity for Bitcoin miners, analysts say. IREN\u2019s future now rests on transforming into a hyperscale AI cloud operator, rather than mining Bitcoin as it had done in the past, according to wealth management firm Bernstein. Bernstein slashed its price target on IREN to $100 from $125 per share but maintained its \u201coutperform\u201d rating, citing the successful pivot to building its AI cloud business. \u201cIREN remains our top pick among AI focused Bitcoin miners,\u201d analyst Gautam Chhugani and his three colleagues said in a note shared with DL News. Bernstein\u2019s positive rating lands as the cryptocurrency industry is still reeling from a $1.5 trillion drawdown since October despite stocks and other assets"

Shop the Plan \u2192 Product Pricing Company Resources Blog Sign In Free Sign Up Free Sign Up EN Stock Reviews IREN Missed Revenue Estimates by 18% Last Quarter. Here Is Why That Might Be the Wrong Number to Watch David Beren \u2022 6 minute read Reviewed by: David Hanson Last updated Apr 27, 2026 Key Stats for IREN Stock 52-Week Range: $5.73 to $76.87 Current Price: $50.64 Street Mean Target: ~$72 TIKR Model Target (Mid): ~$222 Market Cap: $16.8 billion Now Live: Discover how much upside your favorite stocks could have using TIKR\u2019s new Valuation Model (It\u2019s free) >>> What Happened? IREN ( IREN ) started life as a Bitcoin miner, building data centers on cheap renewable energy in British Columbia and Texas. Then management looked at what that same infrastructure could be worth in an AI world and decided to change course entirely . That pivot is now the whole story , and it is why the recent numbers need a little more unpacking than usual. Overall, Q2 fiscal 2026 revenue came in at $184.7 million, missing the $224 million consensus by around 18%. That sounds bad. But adjusted EPS of negative $0.03 beat estimates of negative $0.25 by a wide margin, and free cash flow landed slightly ahead of expectations, too. The revenue gap is mostly a timing issue. Bitcoin mining revenue is falling faster than AI cloud contracts are ramping up, creating an awkward middle period that doesn\u2019t tell you much about where this business is actually going. IREN Earnings Beats and Misses. ( TIKR ) Underneath the messy headline numbers, IREN secured $3.6 billion in GPU financing tied to a Microsoft contract, announced a new 1.6 gigawatt data center campus in Oklahoma, and held its target of $3.4 billion in annualized recurring revenue by the end of 2026. The $540 million capex spend in the quarter tells you management is not slowing down. After a 663% run over the past year, the stock now sits about 34% off its highs. That looks more like transition-quarter noise than anything structural. Value IREN Limited instantly (Free with TIKR) >>> Why the Analyst Community Is Split Right Down the Middle The street target of around $72 implies roughly 42% upside from here, though analyst coverage on IREN is still building. The bulls are genuinely bullish. Bernstein raised its target to $75 earlier this year, while Roth Capital raised its target to $82, pointing to a scenario in which the GPU buildout delivers $2.7 billion in revenue and $1.2 billion in EBITDA by fiscal 2028. The skeptics have a reasonable argument, too. A negative free cash flow of $468 million in a single quarter is real money, and IREN needs capital markets"

Both are building the data centers that power the AI boom. Both are early-stage, burning cash, and priced on future potential rather than today\u2019s profits. But Wall Street doesn\u2019t treat them equally \u2014 and the gap is wider than you might think. Nebius Group ( NBIS ) and Iren Limited ( IREN ) are chasing the same tailwind: surging demand for GPU compute. The question is which one has stronger analyst conviction. The numbers tell a clear story. Nebius is the Amsterdam-listed rebuild of Yandex\u2019s core AI and cloud infrastructure business. After geopolitical events forced its founding team out of Russia in 2022, chief executive Arkady Volozh and hundreds of engineers relocated to Europe and restarted from scratch. Today, Nebius operates a full-stack AI cloud \u2014 think GPU clusters, managed storage, and developer tools \u2014 targeting AI start-ups and enterprises that need compute but can\u2019t afford to build their own. At a Morgan Stanley conference in March 2026, Volozh described the ambition plainly: the company is targeting nearly one gigawatt of active power this year and has a pipeline reserved for up to three gigawatts. \u201cDemand far exceeds our supply,\u201d Nebius Chief Revenue Officer Marc Boroditsky told the audience, according to the conference transcript. Iren is an Australian-founded company that spent seven years as a Bitcoin miner before pivoting hard into AI cloud hosting. It now operates 810 megawatts of existing data centers \u2014 predominantly air-cooled, which is increasingly in demand \u2014 and has secured a $9.7 billion AI"

Bernstein said IREN is shifting from cryptocurrency mining to an AI-focused business and will fully shut down its mining business within the next several years. Bernstein cut its price target on IREN to $100 from $125, while maintaining its Outperform rating . The lower target reflects a reduction in Bitcoin (BTC) mining scale and dilution from a recent share sale , and is unrelated to its AI business outlook . Forecast Trend Report by Period See more mid- to long-term trend analysis IREN is set to pivot from cryptocurrency mining to an AI-focused business, in a shift that reflects broader changes across the industry. Decrypt reported on April 28 that Bernstein said in a report IREN is rapidly transforming into a high-speed AI cloud company and will fully wind down its mining business within the next several years. Bernstein lowered its price target on IREN to $100 from $125, while maintaining its Outperform rating. The firm said the lower target reflects a smaller Bitcoin mining operation and dilution from a recent share sale, not any change in its outlook for the company\u2019s AI business. Investors are also watching a broader shift among mining companies toward AI and data-center businesses. The speed of that transition and changes in profit structure are seen as key variables. The clock is ticking for IREN as it aims to reposition itself as an AI infrastructure player, right when hyperscalers \u2014 those sprawling cloud companies \u2014 are in a mad dash for electricity, semiconductors, and real estate. On Thursday, Applied Digital disclosed a $7.5 billion, 15-year lease deal with an undisclosed U.S. hyperscaler. According to Reuters Breakingviews, Amazon, Microsoft, Alphabet, and Meta could collectively lay out close to $630 billion on data centers and AI chips by 2026. Three commentary pieces in the last two days highlighted sharply divided opinions on IREN. Seeking Alpha flagged the risk that a hefty share-sale \u2014 potentially $6 billion \u2014 might undermine the company\u2019s push into hyperscale. Simply Wall St pointed to analyst skepticism about transforming crypto infrastructure for AI use. Yahoo Finance circled back to IREN following a notable run-up in the stock. IREN on March 4 said it struck a deal to acquire over 50,000 Nvidia B300 GPUs\u2014these are the chips powering AI model training and operation. With that batch, IREN\u2019s targeted GPU capacity jumps to 150,000. Management is projecting the expanded fleet could back more than $3.7 billion in annualized AI cloud revenue by late 2026, based on forecasted pricing and total installed units. The company also noted it had locked in $9.3 billion in funding across the past eight months. Microsoft inked a $9.7 billion deal for cloud services back in November, spelling out a five-year arrangement with deployments rolling out at IREN\u2019s Childress, Texas campus through 2026. By the end of January, IREN counted $2.8 billion in cash and cash equivalents and said it had more than 4.5 gigawatts of power capacity on the grid, boosted by a 1.6-gigawatt addition in Oklahoma. Even so, the company\u2019s latest numbers highlight just how much of the transition is still ahead. For the quarter ending Dec. 31, IREN pulled in $167.4 million from bitcoin mining, while AI Cloud Services brought in just $17.3 million. Back then, co-founder and co-CEO Daniel Roberts described the market as the company\u2019s \u201cstrongest demand environment to date,\u201d but AI was still far from dominating the top line. Nasdaq Back in March, Roberts was clear: \u201cScaling to 150,000 GPUs\u201d could make IREN one of the world\u2019s top AI cloud infrastructure players.     "content": "Bernstein sees IREN pivot to $3.7B AI cloud | Grafa Skip to main content Hub News Signals Market Economy Country Learn Channel Company Livestream Grafa TV Quote Forex Assets Quote Settings United States Australia Crypto Home news crypto iren ai cloud pivot bitcoin mining Bernstein sees IREN pivot to $3.7B AI cloud Bernstein sees IREN pivot to $3.7B AI cloud Written by Heidi Cuthbert Published Apr. 28 2026 Last Updated Apr. 28 2026 Discover more Crypto News Share Bernstein analysts say IREN is pivoting from Bitcoin mining toward AI cloud infrastructure, signalling a major shift in its long-term business model. The firm\u2019s AI cloud division already has around 150,000 GPUs contracted, supporting an estimated $3.7 billion annual revenue run rate once fully operational. A key driver is a long-term agreement with Microsoft, which includes customer prepayments that help fund IREN\u2019s multi-billion-dollar infrastructure buildout. \u201cIREN will eventually sunset the Bitcoin mining business as it retrofits existing sites to accelerate cloud deployment,\u201d Bernstein analysts said. The company is repurposing mining sites in Texas and British Columbia by replacing ASIC rigs with GPUs, redirecting power capacity toward higher-margin AI workloads. Bernstein has assigned IREN a $100 price target, implying nearly 100% upside from current levels below $50, while maintaining an Outperform rating despite a more conservative outlook. The shift reflects a broader trend among miners moving into AI and high-performance computing, as firms seek more stable and profitable revenue streams beyond Bitcoin mining. He called IREN speculative\u2014though he stopped short of outright dismissing the stock. IREN steps into its May 7 quarterly report with investors eyeing evidence that the company\u2019s AI cloud expansion can justify the recent stock re-rating. It\u2019s a packed week ahead for the broader trade, as Microsoft, Meta, Apple, Alphabet, and Amazon are all set to deliver earnings. Investors are bracing for updates on artificial-intelligence spending plans. Jim Cramer telling investors to \u201ctake the gain\u201d on IREN comes right as the AI data-center trade gears up for one of its busiest tests this quarter. Friday, the CNBC host called the market \u201ca beast\u201d when it comes to anything connected to data centers\u2014a category he stretched from Google and Amazon to Meta, Nvidia, and Broadcom. That session saw the Nasdaq jump 1.63%, while the Dow dropped roughly 80 points, highlighting just how much money is piling into the AI infrastructure trade. Internet Archive This shift is significant, as IREN isn\u2019t just a play on its bitcoin-mining past anymore. The stock now hinges on a different bet: demand for AI computing muscle must keep outpacing what it takes to construct data centers, snap up chips, and secure the funding required to keep the engine running. Cramer didn\u2019t issue a clear-cut sell on IREN. In his April 24 recap, he said he was \u201cnot going to veto IREN,\u201d though he described the stock as speculative. He circled back to earlier worries over the company\u2019s fundraising plans\u2014mentioning both an ordinary share sale and a $1 billion convertible bond, which allows debt holders to swap for equity down the line. Insider Monkey The real kicker landed at the close: don\u2019t let greed take over if you\u2019re already sitting on fast profits. Cramer noted his charitable trust cut back its stake in Broadcom\u2014a key player in AI networking and custom chips\u2014and advised anyone whose data-center stocks surged 30% or 40% within a week to lock in some gains Monday. That way, he said, you\u2019ll have cash ready if prices dip. Shares of IREN opened Monday at $50.64, slipping 2.7% from the last close. That price puts the company\u2019s market cap near $15.1 billion. The company describes itself as a vertically integrated AI cloud operator, handling everything from constructing massive data centers to assembling GPU clusters aimed at training and deploying AI models. The latest analyst commentary reinforces AI as the key short term catalyst, while also highlighting that converting crypto infrastructure to AI use is complex and may take longer than investors hope. That timing risk feels increasingly central to the story, even as Bitcoin mining and contract duration remain important watchpoints. The Globe and Mail has not reviewed this content. Please see disclaimer . What Is One of the Best AI Stocks to Buy Right Now? Motley Fool - Mon Apr 27, 11:45AM CDT Key Points IREN is on pace to reach $3.4 billion in annualized revenue, but it could grow significantly more. Following a recent deal with Microsoft, the company is in discussions with hyperscalers to contract its data center capacity. The company's careers page shows it is hiring for key roles in Australia, suggesting an expanding data center pipeline. 10 stocks we like better than Iren \u203a Demand for data centers is booming in 2026. The Motley Fool's research shows artificial intelligence (AI) spending by big tech is expected to increase roughly 50% this year to $600 billion. IREN (NASDAQ: IREN) is one of the best pure-play data center stocks positioned to benefit from this spending boom. Its ownership of fundamental assets, such as land and access to electricity, and its ability to meet construction timelines, have been recognized by investors. Despite the stock rising by over 700% in the past year, its run might be just beginning. Will AI create the world's first trillionaire? Our team just released a report on the one little-known company, called an \"Indispensable Monopoly\" providing the critical technology Nvidia and Intel both need. Continue \u00bb Image source: Getty Images. Management has set a target of reaching $3.4 billion in annualized revenue by the end of 2026. However, this accounts for only 10% of its secured power capacity for its data centers. This suggests the potential to scale to around $30 billion or more over time, making the stock's market cap of about $17 billion look cheap. The company has already secured a $9.7 billion, five-year agreement with Microsoft . The most important part of this deal isn't the value, but that it strengthens IREN's credibility, helping accelerate discussions with other hyperscalers to stack more revenue. While the sustainability of data center spending might be a concern for some investors, Co-CEO Dan Roberts offered an important insight into demand strength during the company's February earnings call. \"We have multiple advanced negotiations underway for larger-scale deployments, and are also seeing hyperscalers and AI enterprises increasingly focus on air-cooled GPUs, given the faster deployment timelines.\" Roberts' comment shows that another deal"

The trajectory has been consistent: build the hyperscale infrastructure that training, agentic AI, and production inference demand while proving performance at every layer. Now, we\u2019re announcing that Nebius has achieved NVIDIA Exemplar Cloud on NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 for training, powered by NVIDIA Blackwell Ultra GPUs . This validates performance against NVIDIA\u2019s reference architecture, meeting the 95% threshold across all benchmarking recipes. What Exemplar Cloud means What Exemplar Cloud means The NVIDIA Exemplar Clouds initiative evaluates cloud providers against real-world AI training workloads using NVIDIA\u2019s Performance Benchmarking recipes. It validates performance, resiliency, and TCO against NVIDIA\u2019s standards \u2014 not synthetic benchmarks. For teams deciding where to run large-scale training, it\u2019s a transparent, independently verified signal that infrastructure will perform as expected. Nebius is among the first cloud providers globally to achieve Exemplar on NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 for training, and one of a small group to hold it across multiple GPU generations . More than a Benchmark More than a Benchmark Exemplar Cloud is one outcome of a collaboration that now spans AI factory design , fleet health monitoring, inference optimizations, and the Physical AI Data Factory Blueprint \u2014 where Nebius was named alongside Azure as a cloud provider in the NVIDIA announcement. The engineering collaboration that drives Exemplar validation is the same collaboration shaping how Nebius builds and operates infrastructure across the full stack \u2014 from cluster acceptance and networking tuning to production inference through Token Factory. \u201cThe NVIDIA Exemplar Cloud achievement is a testament to Nebius\u2019s commitment to delivering world-class AI infrastructure,\u201d said Warren Barkley, Vice President of Product Management at NVIDIA . \u201cAchieving Exemplar across multiple GPU generations, from NVIDIA HGX H200 to NVIDIA GB300 NVL72, reflects the depth of engineering collaboration between our teams and gives developers and enterprises confidence that their most demanding workloads will perform at scale.\u201d \u201cAs we scale into gigawatt-class AI factories and next-generation platforms with NVIDIA, Exemplar Cloud is how we prove the infrastructure performs \u2014 not just at peak specs, but against real-world training workloads at scale,\u201d said Laurelle Roseman, VP of Global Partnerships . \u201cThat confidence comes from our ability to rapidly bring up and validate real-world training and inference workloads, and deliver results customers can trust.\u201d With NVIDIA Vera"

CoreWeave tacked on 8.4%. Nvidia slipped 2.1%. Why it matters now: Meta Platforms, a top Nebius client, bumped its capital spending outlook for 2026 to $125 billion-$145 billion, up from the previous $115 billion-$135 billion, after the U.S. market closed. The company pointed to pricier components and additional data center expenses. Capital expenditures cover big-ticket items like servers, network equipment, and buildings. For Nebius, this isn\u2019t simply a general AI play. Back in March, the company announced Meta would purchase $12 billion in dedicated capacity starting early 2027\u2014and if Nebius can\u2019t line up other buyers, Meta has the option to add as much as $15 billion more over five years. That puts the maximum potential value of the deal at $27 billion. Investors are left weighing if Nebius can turn its hefty backlog into usable capacity before expenses spiral. In February, the company posted fourth-quarter 2025 revenue at $227.7 million\u2014a 547% jump\u2014while net loss from continuing operations came in at $249.6 million. For 2025, property and equipment purchases soared to $4.07 billion. Nebius lined up $4.3375 billion in March through a convertible-note offering to help fund the expansion. The company said the money is earmarked for data centers, GPUs, its full-stack AI cloud and scaling up its presence. Convertible notes, as a reminder, are debt that can be swapped for equity down the line. After the debt raise, Tom Blackwell, chief communications officer at Nebius, told Reuters the company is \u201cwell-funded\u201d for its $16 billion to $20 billion capital spending plans set for 2026. Large customer contracts, he added, can serve as \u201ca very efficient source of capital\u201d with the right structuring. Reuters The expansion is picking up pace on both sides of the Atlantic. On March 31, Nebius announced plans for a 310-megawatt AI factory in Lappeenranta, Finland, aiming to bring its first capacity online by 2027. CEO Arkady Volozh described the project as a \u201csignificant addition\u201d to Nebius\u2019s global infrastructure play. Nebius Nebius isn\u2019t just pushing GPU hours anymore. With its AI Cloud 3.5 update, the company rolled out serverless AI, so developers can spin up workloads without touching the backend infrastructure. GPU choices for inference also got a boost\u2014users can access more options when it\u2019s time for a trained model to generate results. Nvidia\u2019s partnership adds a fresh angle. On March 11, Nvidia announced a $2 billion investment in Nebius and detailed plans to collaborate on future hyperscale cloud"

    "content": "Nebius Group (NASDAQ: NBIS) Stock Price Rises 4.2% Amid Q1 Earnings and AI Infrastructure Momentum About Home Politics Economy & Business US News Select Page Economy & Business \u00b7 Featured \u00b7 News & Analysis --> Nebius Group (NASDAQ: NBIS) Stock Price Rises 4.2% Amid Q1 Earnings and AI Infrastructure Momentum By Timothy Mason | Apr 30, 2026 | Economy & Business , Featured , News & Analysis Shares in Nebius Group (NASDAQ: NBIS), the Amsterdam based AI infrastructure company, gained around 4.2 percent in a single session as the market assessed its Q1 2026 earnings release and continued momentum across the company\u2019s growing contract backlog. The stock had entered the earnings day having already surged roughly 87 percent year to date, establishing itself as one of the top performing names on the Nasdaq amid sustained investor appetite for AI infrastructure plays. Analysts had forecast a loss of $0.81 per share for the quarter on revenue of approximately $375 million, representing a substantial sequential jump from the $227.7 million reported in Q4 2025, a quarter in which the company missed earnings expectations. Nebius operates full stack AI infrastructure including large scale GPU clusters and cloud platforms, and counts Nvidia as a strategic backer following a $2 billion equity investment from the chipmaker. The company\u2019s contracted revenue backlog has grown to approximately $46 billion, anchored by a five year commitment worth up to $27 billion from Meta Platforms for dedicated AI cloud capacity and a separate $19.4 billion commitment from Microsoft for GPU compute from its New Jersey data centre. Nebius raised $4 billion through a convertible debt offering to fund its aggressive $16 to $20 billion capital expenditure plan for 2026, with management describing the company as now well funded to meet its spending targets. Full year 2026 revenue guidance stands at $3 to $3.4 billion , up from $530 million in 2025, implying a roughly sixfold expansion in revenue if the company executes on its buildout schedule. The stock carries a consensus Strong Buy rating from 12 analysts covering the name, with an average 12 month price target of $163, implying meaningful upside from recent trading levels. Sceptics point to extreme valuation multiples, with the stock trading at around 47 times trailing sales, significant insider selling of approximately $14.7 million worth of shares over the past 90 days, and a history of missing revenue estimates in three of the past four quarters. Wolfe Research initiated coverage with a Peer Perform rating, acknowledging the demand story as proven but flagging execution and financing risk given the scale of capital deployment still required to complete the 2026 buildout. The update introduces support for NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server Edition GPUs for high end AI processing. A new Data Transfer Service is launching alongside Aether to simplify enterprise data migration into Nebius cloud regions. Nebius Group, trading as NasdaqGS:NBIS, is rolling out what it describes as its most significant AI cloud upgrade in months with the Aether release. The news arrives as the shares trade around $147.16, with a very large 1 year return and a 63.6% gain year to date. Even with a 7.5% decline over the past week, the stock is still up 46.0% over the past 30 days, which keeps recent momentum in focus for investors. For readers watching how AI infrastructure providers position themselves, Aether adds a mix of serverless AI, new GPUs and data migration tools that may affect how enterprises view Nebius as a platform choice. The update may be relevant to how Nebius connects recent share price moves with long term customer adoption and revenue growth, so upcoming usage trends and customer announcements around Nebius AI Cloud 3.5 will be key data points to monitor. Serverless AI lets customers spin up inference or training jobs without configuring clusters, which could appeal to teams currently tied to more manual setups on providers like Amazon Web Services, Microsoft Azure, or Google Cloud. Adding NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server Edition GPUs broadens Nebius\u2019s hardware menu alongside the existing NVIDIA HGX B300 clusters that TD SYNNEX has already reserved on Nebius AI Cloud. This gives enterprises and partners a clearer path from trial projects to heavier production workloads. The new Data Transfer Service also addresses a practical hurdle, since moving large datasets into a new AI-focused cloud can be both time-consuming and risky if tooling is immature.     "content": "Nebius Group (NBIS) Pulls Back to $147.16 as $4.34 Billion Debt Raise and Finland Factory Anchor Long-Term Bull Case About Home Politics Economy & Business US News Select Page Economy & Business \u00b7 Featured \u00b7 News & Analysis --> Nebius Group (NBIS) Pulls Back to $147.16 as $4.34 Billion Debt Raise and Finland Factory Anchor Long-Term Bull Case By Ewan Scott | Apr 26, 2026 | Economy & Business , Featured , News & Analysis Nebius Group N.V. ( NASDAQ: NBIS ) closed at $147.16 on Friday April 24, falling 6.32 percent on the session in a pullback that erased a portion of the extraordinary gains accumulated across one of the most remarkable stock runs in the public markets this year, with the after-hours price recovering modestly to $146.01 as lighter buying emerged to partly absorb the day\u2019s selling. The Friday decline does little to dent a performance that remains staggering in context: NBIS has delivered approximately 659 percent appreciation over the past 52 weeks, rising from a low of $20.25 to an April high of $166.10, a move that has taken the company from a relatively obscure European AI infrastructure play to one of the most discussed neocloud names among institutional and retail investors globally. The week itself was defined by two major catalysts that reinforce the long-term investment case even as Friday\u2019s session reflected the natural weight of profit-taking on a stock trading at its highest levels ever: Nebius closed a $4.34 billion convertible debt funding round that a company executive described as leaving the company \u201cwell-funded\u201d to meet its 2026 capital spending requirements, and announced plans to build a 310 megawatt AI factory in Finland at a total investment of approximately $10 billion. The Finland project, which would rank among the largest AI infrastructure facilities in Europe, extends Nebius\u2019s geographic footprint beyond its existing clusters in the US and Israel and addresses the growing demand among European enterprises, cloud users, and AI developers for compute infrastructure that complies with European data residency and regulatory frameworks without being dependent on US or Asian hyperscale facilities. What distinguishes Nebius from pure infrastructure peers like CoreWeave (CRWV) is the full-stack nature of its offering: rather than simply providing GPU capacity, Nebius has built a platform encompassing data ingestion, model training, inference, deployment, and developer tooling on top of its physical GPU clusters, creating a stickier customer relationship and a potential path to higher-margin service revenue as the platform matures. Reports from The Information this week indicated Nebius is in active discussions to acquire Israeli AI startup AI21 Labs, a natural language processing model developer whose technology would extend Nebius\u2019s capability set into foundational model ownership and position the company even further from the pure infrastructure commodity category if a deal is completed. Nebius , Hits Nebius Hits $375M in Q1 Revenue as Goldman Lifts Target to $205 on Meta Deal Stability 30.04.2026 - 03:53:12 | boerse-global.de Nebius posts $375M Q1 revenue, positive EBITDA, and a $50B backlog, but faces a $7.2B financing gap amid massive data center expansion. Nebius Hits $375M in Q1 Revenue as Goldman Lifts Target to $205 on Meta Deal Stability - Foto: \u00fcber boerse-global.de The numbers coming out of Nebius are getting harder to ignore. The AI infrastructure player posted $375 million in revenue for the first quarter of 2026, landing squarely within analyst expectations, while its stock closed Tuesday at $141.19 \u2014 up 4.2 percent and hovering near all-time highs. The quarterly print marks a sharp acceleration from the $227.7 million recorded in the prior quarter, though the company reported a loss of $0.81 per share. Goldman Sachs responded by raising its price target to $205, citing the stability of Nebius's $12 billion dedicated compute contract with Meta \u2014 part of a potential $27 billion total package \u2014 and projected revenue growth extending through 2030. The bank flagged the simultaneous management of nine data center construction projects as a key risk, noting that the operational complexity leaves virtually no margin for error. A $50 Billion Backlog Meets a $7.2 Billion Gap Nebius is sitting on a massive order book of nearly $50 billion through 2031, underpinned by major deals with Microsoft worth $19.4 billion and the Meta contract. NVIDIA has also injected $2 billion in strategic investment, bolstering the company's liquidity position. Should investors sell immediately? Or is it worth buying Nebius? But the scale of the ambition creates its own math problem. Nebius plans to invest between $16 billion and $20 billion this year alone to expand power capacity beyond 3 gigawatts \u2014 with nearly 1 gigawatt expected to be available by year-end. Against roughly $10 billion in available liquidity, that leaves a financing gap of about $7.2 billion. Investors will be watching closely for how management intends to bridge that shortfall. Margins Turn Positive as Software Ambitions Grow There are signs the operational picture is improving. Nebius reported its first positive adjusted EBITDA of $15 million in the prior quarter, and management is targeting a margin of roughly 40 percent for the"

CTech 09:26, 09.04.26 AI21 Labs is in acquisition talks once again, this time with AI infrastructure company Nebius, according to a report by The Information, months after earlier negotiations with Nvidia failed to result in a deal. Calcalist previously reported that the Israeli startup had held advanced discussions with Nvidia at a reported valuation of $2 billion to $3 billion, above its last known valuation of $1.4 billion from 2023. Those talks reached senior levels but ultimately did not materialize into a transaction. 1 View gallery AI21 Labs founders. ( Photo: Orel Cohen ) In January, co-founder and chairman Amnon Shashua confirmed that AI21 was engaged in discussions with Nvidia as well as other potential buyers, indicating that the company was formally exploring strategic options. Founded in 2017 by Shashua, Professor Yoav Shoham and Ori Goshen, AI21 was among the early companies developing large-scale language models. However, it has struggled in recent years to keep pace with leading AI developers such as OpenAI and Anthropic. The company has since narrowed its focus to enterprise applications, emphasizing accuracy and reliability. It discontinued development of its consumer product, Wordtune, and now centers on offerings such as its Maestro platform and a recently introduced reasoning model. Annual revenue is estimated at around $50 million. Related articles: \u201cThere are talks with Nvidia and others\u201d: Shashua confirms AI21 acquisition discussions AI21 never closed its reported $300 million round as Nvidia weighs an acquisition Nvidia in advanced talks to acquire AI21 in $2-3 billion deal focused on talent AI21 employs roughly 200 people, down from about 260 in 2023, after scaling back expansion plans. Much of the interest in the company has been attributed to its workforce, which includes highly trained AI researchers. The earlier Nvidia talks were widely viewed as driven primarily by this talent base, rather than by AI21\u2019s commercial scale. Nebius, by contrast, has been expanding its presence in enterprise AI infrastructure. Earlier this year, it acquired"

    "published": "2026-04-01T07:00:00Z",
    "content": "MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0: Top-tier AI performance on NVIDIA Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra Search Contact sales Log in to Token Factory Log in to AI Cloud AI Cloud Token Factory Solutions Why Nebius Pricing Docs Resources Company For investors Blog / Platform news / MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0: Top-tier AI performance on NVIDIA Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra April 1, 2026 14 mins to read Share Today we\u2019re announcing our latest submission to the MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0 benchmark suite, the industry-standard benchmark for measuring AI inference performance developed by MLCommons\u00ae. In this round, Nebius continued to demonstrate strong results for AI inference workloads running on the latest NVIDIA platforms. Our submission includes systems built on NVIDIA Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra GPUs \u2014 NVIDIA HGX B200, NVIDIA HGX B300 and the rack-scale NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 system. We benchmarked three models in this round: DeepSeek R1, Qwen3-VL 235B and gpt-oss 120B. Two of these models, Qwen3-VL 235B, a multimodal model capable of understanding both visual and text inputs; and gpt-oss 120B, a large open-source language model, were introduced to the MLPerf\u00ae Inference benchmark suite in this release. Together with DeepSeek R1, these benchmarks represent the type of frontier-scale models increasingly used in modern AI applications. The MLPerf\u00ae benchmark suite continues to evolve alongside the AI industry, introducing new workloads that reflect the latest trends in large language and multimodal models. Our results in this round demonstrate Nebius\u2019 readiness to run these frontier-scale workloads on cutting-edge AI infrastructure. Ready for frontier model inference Ready for frontier model inference In the MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0 round, Nebius benchmarked three NVIDIA AI systems built on the latest Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra architectures: NVIDIA HGX B200 NVIDIA HGX B300 NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 Overall, Nebius achieved 10 first-place results out of 16 benchmark submissions, along with other vendors submitting results. We also submitted five additional use cases where we were the only submitter, so we do not count them as first-place results. Below is the table with all 21 submissions: Submission results * HGX B200 (8 Blackwell GPUs) HGX B300 (8 Blackwell Ultra GPUs) GB300 NVL72 (1 Blackwell Ultra GPU) GB300 NVL72 (8 Blackwell Ultra GPUs) GB300 NVL72 (72 Blackwell Ultra GPUs) DeepSeek R1 (server) 51,693 / 1st [1] 60,413 / 1st [2] \u2014 64,510 / 1st [3] 575,580 / 1st [4] DeepSeek R1 (offline) 58,582 / 1st [5] 69,319 / 3rd [6] \u2014 76,347 / 2nd [7] 673,936 / 1st [8] Qwen3-VL 235B (server) \u2014 45 / \u2014 [9] 43 / 1st [10] \u2014 \u2014 Qwen3-VL 235B (offline) \u2014 78 / \u2014 [11] 61 / 3rd [12] \u2014 \u2014 gpt-oss 120B (server) 87,444 / 1st [13] 100,437 / 5th [14] 14,973 / \u2014 [15] \u2014 1,096,770 / 1st [16] gpt-oss 120B (offline) 85,921 / 2nd [17] 106,885 / 5th [18] 15,372 / \u2014 [19] \u2014 1,046,150 / 1st [20] gpt-oss 120B (interactive) 13,155 / \u2014 [21] \u2014 \u2014 \u2014 \u2014 * Performance units: Tokens per second for LLM benchmarks (DeepSeek R1, gpt-oss 120B); queries per second for Qwen3-VL 235B (server);"

Nebius , Faces Nebius Faces a $7.2 Billion Chasm as Wall Street Splits on the AI Builder's Grand Ambitions 30.04.2026 - 16:33:11 | boerse-global.de Nebius beats Q1 revenue estimates with $375M, but faces a $7.2B capex gap for nine new data centers. Analysts split as Meta and Microsoft back $50B backlog. Nebius Faces a $7.2 Billion Chasm as Wall Street Splits on the AI Builder's Grand Ambitions - Foto: \u00fcber boerse-global.de The numbers coming out of Nebius Group are the kind that make investors stop and stare \u2014 and then reach for their calculators. The AI infrastructure provider posted first-quarter 2026 revenue of roughly $375 million, a figure that beat analyst expectations, and its stock responded by climbing to $141.19 on Wednesday. But beneath that headline number lies a story of staggering ambition and equally staggering risk. Nebius is sitting on a cash pile of nearly $2.5 billion, with virtually no debt to speak of. That sounds like a fortress balance sheet \u2014 until you consider what management plans to do with it. The company has earmarked between $16 billion and $20 billion in capital expenditures for 2026 alone, funding the construction of nine new data centers across the US and Europe. The gap between what Nebius has and what it needs is roughly $7.2 billion. That chasm has split the analyst community clean down the middle. Freedom Capital Markets recently downgraded the stock to \"Hold,\" pointing to the lofty valuation after a blistering run-up. Wolfe Research initiated coverage with a \"Peer Perform\" rating, acknowledging that demand for Nebius's services is \"proven and low-risk\" while warning that execution and financing risks around the capex plan are substantial. A Seeking Alpha analysis went further, slapping a Sell rating on the stock in late April, arguing that Nebius needs to deliver $3.0 billion to $3.4 billion in revenue alongside a 40% adjusted EBITDA margin \u2014 all while grappling with customer concentration, aggressive convertible note issuance, and an unproven moat. On the other side of the fence, the optimists see a company that has already locked in the demand to justify its spending spree. Goldman Sachs raised its price target to $205, citing a blockbuster deal with Meta that secures long-term revenue and justifies the premium valuation. Analysts on average"

Micah Zimmerman, The Motley Fool Mon, April 20, 2026 at 12:42 AM UTC 0 Key Points An up to $50 billion contracted backlog with Microsoft and Meta Platforms gives Nebius rare visibility into future revenue. The company is spending big and risks can't be ignored. 10 stocks we like better than Nebius Group \u203a There's a version of the AI infrastructure story that almost everyone knows: Nvidia (NASDAQ: NVDA) makes the chips, the hyperscalers build the data centers, and the software companies build on top. But the middle layer in that stack -- which is the specialized cloud providers who rent GPU compute to companies building artificial intelligence (AI) models -- has one company with a contracted backlog so large relative to its current size that it's hard not to stop and look. Nebius Group (NASDAQ: NBIS) is not a traditional start-up. It emerged in 2024 from the restructuring of Yandex , the Russian internet giant. Its founding CEO, Arkady Volozh -- who built Yandex into a company handling billions of search queries -- walked into this venture with hundreds of experienced infrastructure engineers, $2.5 billion in capital, and decades of experience building and operating large-scale data center systems. Will AI create the world's first trillionaire? Our team just released a report on the one little-known company, called an \"Indispensable Monopoly\" providing the critical technology Nvidia and Intel both need.     "content": "Exclusive: TD Synnex Nabs Over 1,000 Nvidia GPUs For Channel In Nebius Cloud Deal Exclusive: TD Synnex Nabs Over 1,000 Nvidia GPUs For Channel In Nebius Cloud Deal TD Synnex officials Francisco Criado and Jack Donato tell CRN that the Nebius partnership will help channel partners gain access to high-end GPUs in the cloud, which have become a scarce resource due to high demand from AI-native companies. TD Synnex has reserved over 1,000 high-end Nvidia GPUs in the cloud to help solution providers overcome supply constraints and get their customers moving on AI projects. The U.S. distribution giant, which won Nvidia\u2019s Distribution Partner of the Year award for the Americas last month , made the cloud commitment for AI computing clusters based on Nvidia\u2019s B300 GPU with neocloud provider Nebius. TD Synnex announced the deal on Tuesday at its High-Growth Conference in Puerto Rico. [Related: Nvidia Channel Chief: Neoclouds Represent \u2018Next Phase Of Opportunity\u2019 For Partners ] The Fremont, Calif.-based company is accepting purchase orders for B300-based instances through Nebius now, with availability expected to begin in July. The agreement lasts for a year, and it gives TD Synnex the option for renewal. Nvidia Americas channel chief Craig Weinstein told CRN last month that the company sees the partnership as an important way to reach enterprise customers. \u201cIt means that we have an [Nvidia Cloud Partner] that\u2019s going to offer cloud capability to the ecosystem to ensure [that] enterprises can iterate and solution providers have a platform to leverage,\u201d he said. TD Synnex-Nebius Deal Seen As Way To Combat Supply Scarcity In an exclusive interview with CRN, TD Synnex officials Francisco Criado and Jack Donato said that the Nebius partnership will help channel partners gain access to what has become a scarce resource due to high demand from AI-native companies. \u201cThere\u2019s literally no capacity available right now,\u201d said Criado, senior vice president of cloud, security and AI at TD Synnex. TD Synnex expects the Nebius deal to benefit many of its strategic partners who otherwise \u201cwouldn\u2019t get the prioritization that they would need to gain access to the GPUs,\u201d according to Donato, senior director AI and neocloud vendor solutions at TD Synnex. \u201cOur ability to give them that prioritization up there with the general hyperscalers and neocloud providers just gives them access, in general,\u201d he said. \u201cCould they get the same capacity direct? Maybe, but there\u2019s no availability.\u201d TD Synnex: Deal Represents First Of Its Kind In Disty World According to TD Synnex, the Nebius engagement \u201cmarks the first time a global IT distributor has reserved a dedicated Nvidia AI factory-grade cluster from an AI-native cloud provider.\u201d \u201cIt\u2019s not your typical distribution go-to-market where you\u2019re buying and reselling something,\u201d Criado said. \u201cWe\u2019re actually committing and we\u2019ve purchased this reserved instance within the Nebius data center ... and we will now work with our partners and their customers to [drive consumption].\u201d Based on Nvidia\u2019s Blackwell Ultra architecture, the B300 debuted last year as the company\u2019s flagship GPU for x86-based servers that"

Recently, I asked one of our engineers how many lines of code he had manually written in the last six months. His answer: not one. Not because the work had slowed, but because it had accelerated beyond what traditional coding workflows could support. AI had taken over the authoring. He was focused on architecture, validation, and shipping faster than before. \u200d That exchange points to something important about where enterprise AI is heading. Enterprise spending on generative AI grew from $11.5 billion in 2024 to $37 billion in 2025 , a more than threefold increase in a single year. Token demand has risen 28 times since the end of 2024. The infrastructure underneath all of that is no longer a procurement decision; it is a strategic one. \u200d The companies best positioned to compete in the AI era are not necessarily the largest. They are the ones built specifically for AI: no legacy constraints, no accumulated service debt from a pre-GPU era, and crucially, no separation between what they sell and how they operate. \u200d At Nscale, those three properties, AI-native design, full-stack vertical ownership, and internal use of our own platform, are decisions we made from day one. Why legacy cloud was never built for AI Hyperscalers started with traditional cloud. That origin shapes everything that followed. Years of service layering and optimization for general-purpose workloads have produced platforms that are, by design, comprehensive rather than"

Sign up for newsletters Sign up for the digital issue Light Dark Auto All topics Business books Climate & sustainability Consumer & retail Energy Entertainment & media Healthcare Inside the Mind of the CxO Leadership Manufacturing Reinvention & transformation Strategy Tech & innovation The s+b 10 principles collection Thought leaders Value Creation Workforce Young profs The issue Current issue Past issues Sign up The podcast Follow s+b Follow s+b on LinkedIn Follow s+b on Twitter Follow s+b on Facebook Follow s+b on YouTube Gets updates via RSS s+b on the Apple App store s+b on the Google Play store Light Dark Auto Tech & innovation How neocloud Nscale is navigating the AI infrastructure boom The company\u2019s Global President for AI Infrastructure, Nidhi Chappell, describes how demand for data centers is pushing the industry to find new ways to build, scale, and perform at new levels. by David DeLallo April 15, 2026 Share to: Share on X Share on LinkedIn Share on Facebook Email this article Courtesy of Nscale AI is no longer a side bet for most enterprises\u2014it\u2019s quickly becoming core to how products are built, decisions are made, and work gets done. That shift is colliding with a hard physical reality: the compute behind modern AI runs hot, dense, power-hungry\u2014and remains scarce. It\u2019s pushing data center operators to deliver far more capacity, far faster, often with projects measured in the hundreds of megawatts and timelines that feel closer to months than years. Related insights Perspectives from the Global Telecom Outlook, 2025\u20132029 by Florian Gr\u00f6ne Industrial redevelopment offers an accelerated path for building data centers by Markus Sch\u00f6rghuber and Folker Trepte How to fund the data-center boom? The result is a new infrastructure arms race, with \u2018neoclouds\u2019 emerging alongside hyperscalers to deliver AI-first capacity. And that arms race is attracting massive amounts of capital. Data center investment has surged into the hundreds of billions of dollars annually, with PwC estimating that meeting capacity requirements will require about US$2 trillion in capex by 2030. The financing playbook is evolving in kind. New deal structures are emerging, blurring the lines between customer contracts, infrastructure finance, and hardware supply\u2014and raising new questions about risk sharing and resilience. Nidhi CHAPPELL has had a front-row seat to how these dynamics are reshaping strategy. She currently serves in the C-suite at Europe-based neocloud Nscale as Global President of AI Infrastructure, and previously held a senior AI infrastructure leadership role at Microsoft Azure. August 2025 Conferences GasCon 2026 2025 Master Class Series 2026 Upcoming Events School of Energy Calgary Encore Speaking Engagements Recent & Upcoming Engagements Sponsorship Opportunities Consulting About About RBN Careers FAQs History Timeline The Company Our Team Networkers Our Contributors Join the Network Media In the News Sponsorship Sponsor an Event Media Kit Sponsored Content Contact Us Holiday Calendar New Releases Your Cart Breadcrumb Daily Energy Posts Blog (They\u2019ve Got) The Power \u2013 Nscale\u2019s Monarch Data Center Aims to Optimize the Btu-to-GPU Value Chain Thursday, 04/23/2026 Published by: Jason Lindquist Product Highlight NATGAS Appalachia Natural Gas The rapid expansion of data centers and AI applications is one of the biggest stories in the energy sector and the Monarch Compute Campus, which is beginning to take shape in West Virginia, figures to be one of the most interesting sites coming online and could be a compelling test case for future development. In today\u2019s RBN blog, we explore the Monarch project, a massive data center with on-site power generation that seeks to optimize the Btu-to-GPU value chain through upstream access to Appalachian natural gas, creation of the nation\u2019s first state-certified microgrid, and a modular approach to development. Let\u2019s start with the basics. London-based Nscale, which builds, owns and operates data centers, announced March 16 that it had acquired American Intelligence & Power Corp. (AIP), which was established earlier this year by 8090 Industries and our friends at Fidelis New Energy. Since AIP owned the Monarch campus, Nscale\u2019s acquisition included that site. Nscale also said the same day that it had signed a letter of intent to provide 1.35 gigawatts (GW) of compute capacity to Microsoft, setting up the campus as a flagship development of Nvidia\u2019s next-generation Vera Rubin AI platform. We\u2019ll touch on more details about the campus later, but construction is expected to start soon with operations beginning in H2 2027. Should the site expand as planned, it would reach 8.1 GW of capacity by 2031. As we noted in Win, Lose or Draw , numerous factors go into deciding where to build a data center, and each location has its own strengths"

    "content": "AI services without the cost trade-offs | Nscale Nscale Raises $2 Billion in Series C \u2014 the Largest in European History See More Data Centers Nscale data centers Glomfjord Norway Narvik Norway Loughton United Kingdom Texas United States Partner-run data centers Sines Portugal Keflavik Iceland AVAILABLE DATA CENTERS Stavanger Norway Oslo Norway Bl\u00f6ndu\u00f3s Iceland Slough United Kingdom North Carolina United States West Virginia United States All Data Centers Texas, USA Services Explore our full AI stack All Services AI services Inference Endpoints Run models via API Prompt Workbench Test and iterate prompts Fine-tuning Adapt models to your data Platform Services Managed Slurm Distributed model training Kubernetes service Run containerised workloads Instances Provision virtual machines INFRASTRUCTURE SERVICES Compute On-demand GPU & CPU Networking Connect & secure resources Storage Store and access data Fleet OPERATIONS Control Center Manage your environment Observability Monitor systems and jobs Radar API Track usage and events Use our services on demand Start Building Company Company About Nscale Careers Resources Resources Newsroom Blog Docs Media kit Contact Login Data Centers Glomfjord Narvik Loughton Texas Sines Keflavik Stavanger Oslo Bl\u00f6ndu\u00f3s Slough North Carolina West Virgnia Services AI Services Platform Services Infrastructure Services Fleet Operations Company About Careers Resources Newsroom Blog Docs Media Kit Contact Blogs / AI services without the cost trade-offs Industry AI services without the cost trade-offs Eyal Lantzman Abigail MacKenzie-Armes Oscar Savolainen April 24, 2026 \u2022 4 minutes Eyal Lantzman Abigail MacKenzie-Armes Oscar Savolainen April 24, 2026 \u2022 4 minutes Share How to deploy sovereign AI without compromising speed, control, and cost. AI developers and engineers are being forced into a trade-off that shouldn\u2019t exist: move fast with managed AI services and give up control, or build on raw infrastructure and absorb the full cost of complexity. \u200d This is becoming one of the defining bottlenecks of enterprise AI adoption. \u200d Enterprise spending on generative AI has more than tripled year-over-year , while token usage and inference demand are growing at unprecedented rates. Gemini alone saw token usage increase 50x and inference is expected to account for more than half of total AI compute by 2030, according to McKinsey. At the same time, studies from firms like Deloitte consistently point to the same constraint: the gap between experimentation and production remains the hardest part of deploying AI at scale. \u200d Teams can prototype quickly, but turning those prototypes into reliable, cost-efficient, and governed systems is still slow, expensive, and operationally complex. For enterprises, that challenge is compounded by the need to meet sovereignty and compliance requirements without sacrificing performance. \u200d The result is a structural mismatch. Model capability is accelerating. Enterprise infrastructure and enterprise tooling are not keeping pace, while sovereignty and compliance concerns continue to grow. \u200d For engineering teams, that mismatch shows up in very real ways: Environments designed for CPU workloads that are not cost effective for GPU workloads Cost of hardware and rate of change mean that they need to compromise in performance and even worse, the value they get out of"

The facility is planned for the Sievari industrial area, owned by the Town of Harjavalta. Fortum is supporting the project as site development partner. \u200d The project is inline with Fortum\u2019s strategic priorities to drive electrification in theNordics. Fortum laid the groundwork for this site development in June 2024 by signing a planning reservation agreement with the Town of Harjavalta. \u200d The agreement covered marketing, zoning, and grid connection support for a potential data center site in Sievari and marked the first phase of advancing the area for future investment. \u200d Fortum has now signed a site securing and development agreement (SSDA) with Nscale and will continue the work initiated in 2024. In parallel, Nscale and the Town of Harjavalta have begun the process toward a preliminary land sale of the Sievari site. Under the agreement, Fortum\u2019s role is to support the smooth progress of Nscale\u2019s project development, including grid connection design and permitting. \u200d The total power capacity of the planned data center is to be determined during the next design and permitting phases. \u200d \u201cThis cooperation reflects our broader strategy to enable low-carbon growth by providing customers with the right conditions to build scalable, sustainable operations in Finland. These types of projects play a key role in driving electrification and digitalisation in our societies,\u201d Jyrki Holappa , Customer Site Development Director at Fortum states. \u200d \u201cThe Nordic market, and Finland specifically, offers unique advantages that align perfectly with our"

Microsoft will take up the spare capacity at the data center. OpenAI said it was in discussions with Microsoft to rent the compute from the company. OpenAI has moved to temper expectations of its spending plans as a potential IPO looms this year. OpenAI has abandoned plans to rent compute capacity directly from a Norwegian data center, days after confirming it paused a similar project in the U.K. Microsoft is taking the extra compute previously earmarked for OpenAI at a planned 230MW \"Stargate Norway\" facility in Narvik. OpenAI is now in discussions to rent capacity from Microsoft instead, a spokesperson for the company told CNBC. The AI company said in 2025 it had the opportunity to be an \"initial offtaker\" at the data center, which OpenAI positioned under the umbrella of its \"Stargate\" infrastructure project and was being built by UK AI cloud startup Nscale. OpenAI was in discussions to rent around half of the facility's capacity, a source with firsthand knowledge of the matter told CNBC. The source said that Nscale and OpenAI ultimately did not agree on an offtake deal, with Microsoft stepping in to take up the capacity. Bloomberg reported on Tuesday that OpenAI didn't conclude its offtake talks at the data center with Nscale. An OpenAI spokesperson declined to comment on the amount of capacity it discussed renting as part of a potential offtake deal.     "link": "https://news.google.com/rss/articles/CBMiTkFVX3lxTE1OR1RwNk01TTJyRm1FdVpHYkxGbHVJM2dHUjh6Wnk2cjZRQzVKR3FUYlFuZXVOd0h0bVc3RzJHaXFuRDVzMkhpdS0zYnZpUQ?oc=5",
