    "content": "Kimi K2.5 is now available on Nscale | Nscale Nscale Raises $2 Billion in Series C \u2014 the Largest in European History See More Data Centers Nscale data centers Glomfjord Norway Narvik Norway Loughton United Kingdom Texas United States Partner-run data centers Sines Portugal Keflavik Iceland AVAILABLE DATA CENTERS Stavanger Norway Oslo Norway Bl\u00f6ndu\u00f3s Iceland Slough United Kingdom North Carolina United States West Virginia United States All Data Centers Texas, USA Services Explore our full AI stack All Services AI services Inference Endpoints Run models via API Prompt Workbench Test and iterate prompts Fine-tuning Adapt models to your data Platform Services Managed Slurm Distributed model training Kubernetes service Run containerised workloads Instances Provision virtual machines INFRASTRUCTURE SERVICES Compute On-demand GPU & CPU Networking Connect & secure resources Storage Store and access data Fleet OPERATIONS Control Center Manage your environment Observability Monitor systems and jobs Radar API Track usage and events Use our services on demand Start Building Company Company About Nscale Careers Resources Resources Newsroom Blog Docs Media kit Contact Login Data Centers Glomfjord Narvik Loughton Texas Sines Keflavik Stavanger Oslo Bl\u00f6ndu\u00f3s Slough North Carolina West Virgnia Services AI Services Platform Services Infrastructure Services Fleet Operations Company About Careers Resources Newsroom Blog Docs Media Kit Contact Blogs / Kimi K2.5 is now available on Nscale Product Kimi K2.5 is now available on Nscale Dale Chapman April 13, 2026 \u2022 3 minutes Dale Chapman April 13, 2026 \u2022 3 minutes Share Kimi K2.5 brings advanced agentic reasoning, design-to-code capabilities, and production-ready performance to fully managed inference endpoints on Nscale. Kimi K2.5, the 1-trillion-parameter open-weight model that turns screenshots into code, coordinates 100 parallel agents, and outperforms leading closed models on key agentic benchmarks is now available on Nscale Inference Endpoints . \u200d Try Kimi K2.5 on Nscale \u2192 What sets Kimi K2.5 apart Kimi K2.5 from Moonshot AI is an open-weight, natively multimodal model with several architectural choices that set it apart. \u200d It uses a Mixture-of-Experts architecture with 1 trillion total parameters, selectively activating only a subset per request to improve efficiency. The result is frontier-level capability with efficient inference, without compromising performance on real workloads. \u200d Unlike models where vision was added later, Kimi K2.5 was trained from the ground up on 15 trillion mixed visual and text tokens. Images and text are processed as a single unified stream rather than separate pipelines, enabling stronger cross-modal reasoning rather than simple image captioning. \u200d Developers get direct control over model behaviour with thinking mode on Nscale, enabling deeper, multi-step reasoning when needed, with per-request control over the latency and quality trade-off. \u200d Kimi K2.5 in practice Kimi K2.5\u2019s combination of native vision, long-context reasoning, and strong coding performance makes it well suited to workflows where inputs are messy, tasks are multi-step, and outputs need to be production-ready. \u200d Three areas where it stands out below. 1. Design-to-code and visual development What it does: Kimi K2.5 takes a screenshot or mockup and generates fully functional front-end code, including dynamic layouts, scroll"

    "content": "AI roaming: Extending the AI grid | Nscale Nscale Raises $2 Billion in Series C \u2014 the Largest in European History See More Data Centers Nscale data centers Glomfjord Norway Narvik Norway Loughton United Kingdom Texas United States Partner-run data centers Sines Portugal Keflavik Iceland AVAILABLE DATA CENTERS Stavanger Norway Oslo Norway Bl\u00f6ndu\u00f3s Iceland Slough United Kingdom North Carolina United States West Virginia United States All Data Centers Texas, USA Services Explore our full AI stack All Services AI services Inference Endpoints Run models via API Prompt Workbench Test and iterate prompts Fine-tuning Adapt models to your data Platform Services Managed Slurm Distributed model training Kubernetes service Run containerised workloads Instances Provision virtual machines INFRASTRUCTURE SERVICES Compute On-demand GPU & CPU Networking Connect & secure resources Storage Store and access data Fleet OPERATIONS Control Center Manage your environment Observability Monitor systems and jobs Radar API Track usage and events Use our services on demand Start Building Company Company About Nscale Careers Resources Resources Newsroom Blog Docs Media kit Contact Login Data Centers Glomfjord Narvik Loughton Texas Sines Keflavik Stavanger Oslo Bl\u00f6ndu\u00f3s Slough North Carolina West Virgnia Services AI Services Platform Services Infrastructure Services Fleet Operations Company About Careers Resources Newsroom Blog Docs Media Kit Contact Blogs / AI roaming: Extending the AI grid Industry AI roaming: Extending the AI grid Chris Coates April 1, 2026 \u2022 5 minutes Chris Coates April 1, 2026 \u2022 5 minutes Share Explore how Telcos can federate national AI grids into a global AI fabric without losing control. Your phone knows what to do when it crosses a border. Without instruction or intervention, it finds a local cell tower, authenticates, and continues, seamlessly extending the session as you move around outside your home. It is an unremarkable moment, repeated billions of times a day, made possible by decades of inter-carrier agreements, shared standards, and a collective decision by an entire industry to build for interoperability rather than isolation. \u200d Many enterprise AI workloads today are anchored to fixed compute in a specific geography: powerful within their boundaries, but brittle at the edges. When demand spikes in a different region, when a data center fails, or when a regulatory constraint requires local processing, the system either degrades or breaks. The architectural model that underpins most AI deployments was not designed for the operational demands now being placed on it. \u200d AI roaming changes that. It represents the next structural shift in AI infrastructure: the move from sovereign national AI grids to a federated, global AI fabric. \u200d And the technology to make it work already exists. \u200d What AI roaming actually means AI roaming is the ability for an inference workload to move seamlessly across providers and geographies, preserving context and continuity, without the end user or application being aware that a handoff has occurred. It is not simply multi-cloud load balancing. It is stateful, federated inference: the AI equivalent of a cellular handoff between towers. \u200d The architectural distinction matters. In a standard distributed AI"

Catherine Boudreau April 1, 2026 Add Latitude on Google Photo credit: Aerovista Luchtfotografie / Shutterstock West Virginia, with its ready supply of cheap gas and a new law allowing off-grid data centers, may be the next frontier for big-name customers like Microsoft. The hyperscaler in March signed a \u201cletter of intent\u201d to secure nearly 1.4 gigawatts of power from a microgrid project underway in Mason County, W. Va. The developer, Nscale \u2014 a two-year-old startup building AI compute campuses in the U.S. and Europe \u2014 said it plans to deploy hundreds of gas generators by the first half of 2028. The deal marks the first time that Microsoft has committed to a fully off-grid, gas-powered data center at the gigawatt scale, said Olivia Wang, a research analyst at Sightline who\u2019s tracking the global data center pipeline . Previously, she added, the company has mainly used gas generators for backup capacity in new data center builds, not as a primary energy source. Nscale took over the project when it acquired American Intelligence & Power Corporation, which specialized in behind-the-meter microgrids for AI data centers. Stuart Pann, senior advisor at Nscale, told Latitude Media in an interview at CERAWeek that the company saw a lot of opportunity in West Virginia because it sits atop the Marcellus Formation, the largest gas field in the country that spans Appalachia. \u201cWe\u2019re taking advantage of the geographic location of this facility, which can have four separate pipelines fed into it. There\u2019s a chance to make this a gas hub, in the same way Henry Hub is,\u201d Pann said, referring to a gas pipeline interchange in Louisiana that serves as a price benchmark for North America. A Microsoft spokesperson said the company takes a portfolio approach to energy for its global AI infrastructure based on local conditions, grid readiness, and long\u2011term goals. That flexibility allows Microsoft to \u201cbring capacity online faster, ensure reliability, and limit strain on local grids while continuing to invest in"

The initiative focuses on expanding domestic compute capacity while ensuring data stays within national borders. It brings together BT\u2019s estate and connectivity with Nscale\u2019s modular approach to data centre development. Using NVIDIA \u2019s full stack AI platform, the partners aim to support organisations that require high-performance computing without building their own facilities. Nscale plans to deliver up to 14MW of AI data centre capacity across three existing BT sites. BT will provide the underlying infrastructure, including power and network integration, which together form the foundation for scalable AI workloads. Building sovereign AI capacity The development sits within BT Business\u2019 sovereign platform, which allows organisations to run AI workloads entirely within the UK. Hosting compute resources locally means that organisations can meet compliance standards while still accessing advanced processing power. Jon James , CEO, BT Business, says: \u201cThe development of AI and data centre infrastructure is key to unlocking economic growth in the UK, and is only possible with the best, most trusted networks at the foundation. \u201cDigital sovereignty enables UK organisations to adopt the world\u2019s leading technologies, while maintaining resilience and control in a rapidly changing world. \u201cOur collaboration with Nscale reflects BT\u2019s unique position as the digital backbone of the UK \u2013 the only provider with the scale, capabilities and experience to enable the nation\u2019s sovereign ambitions.\u201d Jon James, CEO of BT says AI and data centre infrastructure is key for unlocking the UK's economic growth The facilities are designed to support a range of workloads, from analysing datasets to enabling AI-led applications in sectors such as energy and security. Policy alignment and infrastructure demand The investment aligns with the UK Government\u2019s AI Opportunities Action Plan , which prioritises domestic infrastructure as a means of supporting economic activity and technological capability. Data centres form a core part of this strategy, providing the compute layer required for AI development and deployment. Its own engineers tell a different story 5 No shortcuts, human-review everything, says internal team - and keep hiring junior developers Tim Anderson Wed 29 Apr 2026 // 12:51 UTC Interview Steve Tarcza, director of Amazon Stores, says his team \u2014 StoreGen \u2014 exists to help the retail giant's developers move faster and cut friction. But despite the AI mandate, one principle is non-negotiable: nothing ships without a human checking it first. We can't get to the point where we don't have more junior engineers coming in. We have to continue to grow the talent. We can't end up in a spot where there are not folks to maintain these systems ... The unit focuses not on AWS customers, but on Amazon's internal development teams for its mammoth retail site and operations. Tarcza spoke to us at the AWS London Summit last week, we met him immediately following the keynote at which Alison Kay, VP and managing director UK and Ireland, told attendees that AI technology feels \"like magic.\" Kay cited as an example how the inference engine behind Bedrock, a generative AI service, was rebuilt in 76 days by six engineers, thanks to using the Kiro agentic coding service. \"While the engineers slept, the agents kept building,\" she said, describing how they \"wrote code, tested it, found bugs, fixed them, and deployed it around the clock.\" The Register suggested to Tarcza that there is some trepidation and worry about this, thanks to well-known security and reliability issues with AI. What issues have his team encountered? \"It's the things everybody knows about,\" he tells us. \"It's the hallucinations, it's keeping it within the guardrails.\" There are cases, he said, where the AI is \"even doing work that you didn't ask it to, going further than you wanted to.\" He is an enthusiast for spec-driven development, which was the key feature of Kiro when it was first previewed in July 2025, the idea being that the AI generates a set of tasks for refinement and approval before writing any code. Close TechCrunch Desktop Logo TechCrunch Mobile Logo Latest Startups Venture Apple Security AI Apps Events Podcasts Newsletters Search Submit Site Search Toggle Mega Menu Toggle Topics Latest AI Amazon Apps Biotech & Health Climate Cloud Computing Commerce Crypto Enterprise EVs Fintech Fundraising Gadgets Gaming Google Government & Policy Hardware Instagram Layoffs Media & Entertainment Meta Microsoft Privacy Robotics Security Social Space Startups TikTok Transportation Venture More from TechCrunch Staff Events Startup Battlefield StrictlyVC Newsletters Podcasts Videos Partner Content TechCrunch Brand Studio Crunchboard Contact Us Image Credits: Alex Wong / Getty Images AI OpenAI ends Microsoft legal peril over its $50B Amazon deal Julie Bort 10:40 AM PDT \u00b7 April 27, 2026 On Monday, Microsoft and OpenAI announced that they have, once again, renegotiated the deal binding the two companies. Despite some opinions on X that frame it as a victory for the ChatGPT maker over the Windows giant, both sides are walking away winners. Most importantly, the new terms solve an issue that was hanging over OpenAI\u2019s head since it signed its up-to-$50-billion deal with Amazon. With this new deal, instead of Microsoft having exclusive access to all of OpenAI\u2019s products and IP until the magical day when OpenAI produces AGI, its partnership has a definitive timeline. This contract gives Microsoft a nonexclusive license to OpenAI IP for models and products through 2032. The two companies are still calling Microsoft OpenAI\u2019s \u201cprimary cloud partner,\u201d meaning that the bulk of OpenAI\u2019s cloud will likely be served by Azure for the six years this deal covers, even as OpenAI rushes to build its own data centers with other partners. In October , OpenAI agreed to buy an additional $250 billion worth of Microsoft\u2019s cloud. This line is a message to Microsoft shareholders that OpenAI will still be an enormous Azure customer. OpenAI products will ship \u201cfirst on Azure, unless Microsoft cannot and chooses not to support the necessary capabilities,\u201d the companies say. But, critically, \u201cOpenAI can now serve all its products to customers across any cloud provider.\u201d Again, \u201cfirst\u201d is not defined clearly in this announcement, whether that means exclusive on Azure only for some time period or just that Microsoft will also be among the vendors carrying OpenAI\u2019s latest products. But the most important part of this term: It solves the possibility that Microsoft could sue OpenAI over the AI lab\u2019s deal with Amazon .     "content": "AWS CEO Matt Garman interview: Huge business opportunity in AI-powered software | Fortune Search Subscribe Home Latest Fortune 500 Finance Tech Leadership Lifestyle Rankings Multimedia AI Amazon AWS CEO Matt Garman sees huge business opportunity for Amazon in AI-powered software: \u2018Everything is going to be remade\u2019 By Alexei Oreskovic Alexei Oreskovic Editor, Tech Down Arrow Button Icon By Alexei Oreskovic Alexei Oreskovic Editor, Tech Down Arrow Button Icon April 29, 2026, 2:58 AM ET Add us on Amazon Web Services (AWS) CEO Matt Garman Noah Berger\u2014Getty Images for Amazon Web Services AWS chief executive Matt Garman isn\u2019t losing any sleep over talk of the \u201cSaaSpocalypse.\u201d In fact, he\u2019s so confident companies will continue to buy software-as-a-service in the age of AI that he\u2019s pushing Amazon Web Services into the SaaS business, and rolling out various products aimed directly at office workers and other professionals. Recommended Video On Tuesday AWS unveiled Amazon Quick , a desktop application that lets users interact with an AI chatbot to handle personal productivity tasks like creating work presentations and arranging meetings. The company also announced a trio of new Connect applications to help workers with tasks in specialized fields such as hiring, health care, and supply-chain management. For the world\u2019s leading cloud computing provider, whose core business involves helping companies like Netflix , Adidas , and Pfizer run their websites and operations online, selling software for individual workers is quite a change of pace. In an interview with Fortune on Tuesday, Garman described it as a \u201chuge business opportunity\u201d for AWS and said the advent of agentic AI is what prompted the company to dive in. \u201cAs we look across applications, we see that so many applications are getting done with AI and agents. And we think that there is just such a massive change out there that everything is going to be remade,\u201d Garman said. \u201cI don\u2019t think personal productivity has really been remade for the last 30 years,\u201d Garman said in reference to the new Quick application. Quick will be available to individual users even if they\u2019re not AWS customers, with both a free tier and a premium tier. \u201cThere are going to be millions of successful applications that people us; obviously the vast, vast majority of them will not be built by Amazon or AWS, but we think there are a handful of them that we can build that will be pretty successful and that customers will like,\u201d said Garman. AWS showed off the new products at an event in San Francisco Tuesday in which the company also announced details of a new partnership with OpenAI . For the first time, AWS business customers will be able to integrate OpenAI\u2019s AI models directly into their products and services. Until now, OpenAI\u2019s GPT models were only available via Microsoft\u2019s cloud thanks to an exclusive partnership that Microsoft and OpenAI struck several years ago. That agreement was revised this week, opening the door for AWS to include the latest GPT models and OpenAI\u2019s Codex"

Amazon Connect Decisions is a supply chain planning and intelligence solution that shifts teams from crisis management to proactive planning and decisioning. AI teammates, combining 30 years of Amazon operational science and 25+ specialized supply chain tools, adapt to your business, learn from your team, and continuously improve your operations. Amazon Connect Talent (Preview) is an agentic AI hiring solution built for talent acquisition leaders managing scaled hiring. It delivers AI-led interviews, science-backed assessments, and consistent evaluation, helping recruiters hire high quality candidates faster while providing applicants with a flexible interview experience that reduces human preconceptions. Amazon Connect Customer , previously known as Amazon Connect, delivers intelligent, personalized customer experiences across voice, chat, and digital channels."

    "content": "AWS Partner Updates Put MSP Revenue, AI Services in Focus | news | ChannelE2E Webcasts Resources Sponsors About Contact RSS View Conferences Open Search Bar MSP , Channel partners , Channel partner programs , Cloud migration , Channel technologies AWS Partner Updates Put MSP Revenue, AI Services in Focus April 30, 2026 Share By Todd R. Weiss AWS celebrated its 20th anniversary at its fifth annual Global Partner Summit in March, and the company introduced a wide range of new partner program enhancements inspired by channel partner feedback. The changes are designed to increase partner revenue, sales, and business opportunities. The new partner offerings include a program that delivers direct payments for providing managed services, a new AWS Partner Central Hub consolidating partner co-selling, marketplace, funding, and support in one platform for easier use, and upgrades to the AWS Migration Acceleration Program that now funds new AI-based builds, not just migrations. AWS said the changes reflect direct feedback from partners, who have asked for simpler programs, clearer sales support, and more practical help turning AI demand into customer projects. \u201cPartners said navigating multiple overlapping programs was slowing them down, so we consolidated our Migration Acceleration Program (MAP) into one unified program that now covers everything from migrations to AI new builds, and we made Partner Central the single hub for the entire AWS relationship ,\u201d according to an AWS spokesperson. \u201cPartners told us they needed more support for AI transformation, so we are funding AI new builds through MAP for the first time, launching AI Assessment Funding to help partners turn AI interest into revenue, and introducing the Agentic Process Transformation Initiative with proven methodology and funding to take customers from concept to production in as little as six to eight weeks.\u201d The new program that provides direct cash payments for delivering managed services recognizes \u201cthat ongoing customer management is where the highest partner margins and deepest relationships are built,\u201d the spokesperson said. AWS data shows that MSP-supported customers demonstrate 3.4X higher cloud spend and 58% better retention rates for such services, according to the spokesperson. AWS Makes Right Moves to Help Partners: Analysts Paul Nashawaty , principal analyst for AppDev and modernization at theCUBE Research, told ChannelE2E that he sees AWS\u2019 partner program updates as intentional and meaningfully helpful for partners. \u201cAWS is clearly leaning into incentives that align with where the market is going, including AI-first development, industry-specific solutions, and co-sell alignment,\u201d said Nashawaty. \u201cFrom what we are seeing in theCUBE Research, partners are not just looking for better margins; they want faster paths to revenue, clearer specialization tracks, and stronger field alignment with AWS sellers. These updates check a lot of those boxes by tightening integration between partner programs and AWS\u2019s own go-to-market motion, which ultimately makes it easier for partners to attach services and drive recurring revenue.\u201d And the AI angle from AWS is where this really starts to matter for channel partners like MSPs and VARs, he said. \u201cAWS is lowering the barrier to entry"

    "content": "Crusoe rolls out edge zones as neocloud race shifts toward distributed AI infrastructure | Edge Infrastructure Review AI Infrastructure Cloud Platforms Connectivity Data Centers Edge Computing rss linkedin facebook search search --> About News Companies Markets Directory Guides Resources Events Subscribe Search for: Search X Share X/Tweet Link Comment Crusoe rolls out edge zones as neocloud race shifts toward distributed AI infrastructure Apr 2, 2026 | Stephen Mayhew Categories Digital Infrastructure News | Technology & Architecture Neocloud provider Crusoe recently announced modular AI data centers that provide low-latency, sovereign and rapidly deployable AI infrastructure at the edge. These edge zones deliver AI compute capabilities in location-specific areas based on geography, and the use cases, not supported by traditional hyperscale providers. \u201cCrusoe Edge Zones powered by Crusoe Spark represent the continued expansion of our vertically integrated \u2018 AI Factory \u2019 vision,\u201d says Cully Cavness, co-founder, president, and chief strategy officer of Crusoe. \u201cBy optimizing these modular AI factories to run both the Crusoe Cloud platform and our Managed Inference product, we are delivering a high-performance, distributed solution that provides the speed, sovereignty, and quality that the next generation of AI requires.\u201d Crusoe can quickly deploy new cloud zones, within three months in some instances, expanding cost-saving AI capacity due to its vertically integrated approach. The main use cases are low-latency inference, dedicated enterprise clusters in multiple locations and sovereign AI deployments to serve regulated industries and governments. Crusoe envisions large-scale campuses for model training overhead and modular distributed compute for high-performance edge delivery. The company focuses on driving energy efficient, scalable AI infrastructure in its mission to accelerate energy and intelligence abundance. Organizations can reach out to Crusoe for deployment opportunities if they have geographic expansion, low-latency, or sovereign infrastructure requirements. Should this deal come to fruition, it would more than double Fluidstack\u2019s valuation in a matter of months. In December, the company was reportedly raising around $700 million at a $7.5 billion valuation, sources told Bloomberg at the time, although it didn\u2019t formally announce the close of that round. That round was said to be led by Situational Awareness, an AGI-focused fund founded by former OpenAI researcher Leopold Aschenbrenner, and backed by Stripe\u2019s Collison brothers, former GitHub CEO Nat Friedman, and the AI investor and entrepreneur Daniel Gross. Talks were apparently still ongoing for this round in February, at least with Google, which was considering kicking in $100 million to the round, The Wall Street Journal reported. There\u2019s good reason for the hype over Fluidstack. In November, Anthropic announced that it had signed a $50 billion deal with the startup to build data centers custom-designed for its needs in Texas and New York. Unlike hyperscalers like AWS, which serve all kinds of computing needs, Fluidstack\u2019s infrastructure is built specifically for AI. The deal was a huge vote of confidence for Fluidstack, a company that was relatively unknown in the U.S. Anthropic primarily uses AWS and Google Cloud to serve Claude (though it also has a partnership with Microsoft to supply Claude to that software giant\u2019s customers). But just like rival OpenAI, Anthropic is growing so fast that it needs more capacity, and this deal gives Anthropic more control over its own cloud infrastructure. This partnership is so significant to the startup that Fluidstack \u2014 which was spun out of Oxford and had been a rising star in Europe\u2019s AI scene \u2014 relocated its headquarters from the U.K. to New York. Last month, it also pulled out of a key \u20ac10 billion AI project in France"

From Electrons to Bits\u2122 \u2190 Back to Home Markets Hut 8 Taps $3.25 Billion Debt for Fluidstack-Backed AI Data Center By Wolfie Zhao \u2022 Apr 27, 2026 \u2022 3 min read Hut 8 (NASDAQ: HUT) is seeking to raise $3.25 billion in senior secured notes to fund a large-scale AI data center in Louisiana, the latest addition to a wave of bitcoin miners tapping into the high-yield bond market with infrastructure-style financing backed by long-term compute contracts. The notes, due 2042, will be issued by a Hut 8 subsidiary and used to finance construction of a 245-megawatt data center at the company\u2019s River Bend campus in St. Francisville, according to an April 27 filing. Proceeds will also reimburse prior equity spending and fund reserves tied to the project. The development is anchored by a 15-year, $7 billion lease with Fluidstack, which fully contracts the initial 245 MW of capacity. The agreement also grants Fluidstack a right of first offer for up to an additional 1,000 MW at future phases of the campus, contingent on expanding power availability. Google provides a financial backstop covering obligations for the 15-year base lease term. Illustrative financial disclosures included in the offering materials show the scale of that expected cash generation. The project is modeled to produce about $171 million in revenue in its first full year of operations, ramping steadily to roughly $547 million annually by 2041. Because operating costs are largely passed through or embedded in the lease structure, net operating income closely tracks revenue over the life of the project. After debt service, the data center is projected to generate levered free cash flow of about $37 million in 2027, rising to more than $100 million annually through the early 2030s and reaching roughly $129 million at peak before tapering toward the end of the debt term. The financing is structured at the project level through Hut 8 DC LLC, aligning it more closely with infrastructure and project finance models than the balance sheet-driven borrowing historically used by miners. That shift underscores how publicly traded mining firms are repositioning themselves as developers of AI and high-performance computing infrastructure, as persistently low hashprice and volatile Bitcoin mining economics pressure margins across the sector. In contrast to mining revenue\u2014which fluctuates with network difficulty, transaction fees and Bitcoin prices\u2014the River Bend project is backed by a long-term lease that locks in capacity payments over more than a decade, providing visibility that rating agencies view as critical to supporting investment-grade credit. Credit rating agencies Fitch and S&P Global Ratings assigned the offering a preliminary BBB-, placing the deal at the lowest"

    "content": "FluidStack is in talks to raise $1B at an $18B valuation Skip to content Toggle Navigation News Events TNW Conference June 19 & 20, 2025 All events Spaces Programs Newsletters Partner with us Jobs Contact News Latest Deep tech Sustainability Ecosystems Data and security Fintech and ecommerce Future of work Conference media hub More Startups and technology Investors and funding Government and policy Corporates and innovation Podcast Investors and funding FluidStack is in talks to raise $1B at an $18B valuation April 15, 2026 - 7:14 am Jane Street and Situational Awareness are in discussions to co-lead the round, Bloomberg reports. The Oxford-founded neocloud, which relocated from the UK to the US after landing a $50 billion data centre partnership with Anthropic, has grown its revenue from $1.8M in 2022 to $66.2M in 2024. FluidStack , the AI data centre startup that builds compute infrastructure specifically for AI companies, is in talks to raise approximately $1 billion at a target valuation of $18 billion, Bloomberg reported on Tuesday , citing people briefed on the matter. Trading firm Jane Street and Situational Awareness, an AGI-focused fund founded by former OpenAI researcher Leopold Aschenbrenner and backed by the Collison brothers, former GitHub CEO Nat Friedman, and investor Daniel Gross, are in discussions to co-lead the round. Morgan Stanley is advising on the investment. The $18 billion target valuation would represent a more than doubling of the company\u2019s worth in a matter of months. In December 2025, Bloomberg reported that FluidStack was in discussions to raise around $700 million at a $7.5 billion valuation, in a round said to be led by Situational Awareness; that round did not formally close or receive public confirmation. TNW City Coworking space - Where your best work happens A workspace designed for growth, collaboration, and endless networking opportunities in the heart of tech. Book a tour now The new talks suggest the company\u2019s negotiating position has strengthened considerably since then, almost certainly because of the $50 billion data centre partnership it signed with Anthropic in November 2025. That Anthropic deal , to build custom facilities in Texas and New York , designed specifically to maximise efficiency for Anthropic\u2019s Claude AI workloads, with sites coming online throughout 2026, was the largest infrastructure commitment Anthropic had made independently from cloud providers such as AWS and Google. It represented a defining moment for FluidStack, an Oxford-founded neocloud that had been a notable but relatively unknown presence in European AI infrastructure before the deal. Following its signing, the company relocated its headquarters from the UK to the United States. FluidStack was founded in 2017 as a spin-off from Oxford University and pivoted aggressively towards AI compute infrastructure following the release of ChatGPT in late 2022. Revenue grew from $1.8 million in 2022 to $66.2 million in 2024 as the company built out capacity for AI customers. It manages more than 100,000 GPUs and counts Anthropic, Meta, Poolside, Black Forest Labs, and France\u2019s Mistral among its customers. A $200 million Series A"

    "content": "Fluidstack Stock: The Neocloud With a Stacked Deck Skip to content Access Club Podcast About Expand About Media Contact Terms/Privacy Policy Brokers Pre-IPO Upcoming Radar Expand Enhanced Filter Industries Expand AI Batteries Biotech Climate Cybersecurity Data Infrastructure Defense eCommerce Energy Fintech Food Fusion Gaming Hardware Health Robotics SaaS Social Space Transportation S-1 Feed Search Toggle Menu Search AI \u00b7 Data Infrastructure \u00b7 Hardware Fluidstack Stock: The Neocloud With a Stacked Deck By Craig Stephens Updated: April 29, 2026 This page contains links to our partners. We may be compensated when a link is clicked. Read the disclosures to learn more. Explore opportunities to invest in Fluidstack stock before the IPO. Table of Contents Toggle Notable Fluidstack News About Fluidstack Ownership Funding Rounds Valuation IPO Potential How to Invest in Fluidstack Stock 1. Invest Pre-IPO 2. Participate in the Fluidstack IPO through a broker 3. Buy Fluidstack stock after the IPO Conclusion Fluidstack News Archive Frequently Asked Questions (FAQs) Notable Fluidstack News 04/14/2026: Jane Street to Back Fluidstack at $18B Valuation 02/23/2026: Fluidstack and the GPU Financing Problem 12/04/2025: Fluidstack in Talks for $7B Valuation 11/12/2025: Anthropic invests $50B in American AI infrastructure Older news\u2026 About Fluidstack Fluidstack is an AI cloud platform that supplies dedicated GPU compute infrastructure to frontier AI labs, enterprises, and research organizations. Founded in 2017 at Oxford University, the company started as a marketplace that pooled underused GPU capacity from data centers (the \u201cAirbnb of GPUs\u201d). As demand for large model training accelerated, Fluidstack pivoted into a full-stack neocloud, building and operating dedicated clusters wired together with high-speed networking. Its core offerings center on Atlas OS, an operating system for fast provisioning, and Lighthouse, a monitoring layer that catches and remedies hardware issues. Headquartered in New York City with deep European roots, Fluidstack now manages massive GPU fleets for clients including Anthropic (has pledged a $50 billion investment), Meta, Mistral , and Poolside. It competes with neo-cloud peers such as CoreWeave , Crusoe , Nebius, and Lambda , carving out its niche through tightly managed clusters and aggressive expansion into low-carbon European sites. Ownership Fluidstack is a venture-backed startup owned by its founders, employees, and multiple venture capital firms. Notable venture capital investors include Jane Street, Google, Situational Awareness, Cacti Ventures, Seedcamp, and Mercuri. Funding Rounds Round Date Est. Fluidstack is suddenly one of the most valuable private infrastructure companies in tech, and it's all happening at breakneck speed. The company is currently in advanced talks to raise $1 billion at an $18 billion valuation, sources familiar with the matter told TechCrunch . That's a remarkable 140% increase from the $7.5 billion valuation it secured in its previous round just months earlier. The catalyst? A monster $50 billion agreement with Anthropic to build out dedicated datacenter capacity for the AI company's growing computing needs. The multi-year deal represents one of the largest infrastructure commitments in AI history and signals how seriously frontier model companies are taking compute scarcity. For Anthropic, which competes directly with OpenAI and Google in the large language model space, securing reliable access to GPU clusters isn't just strategic, it's existential. Record $5.7B junk bond highlights rising demand for AI MEXC Exchange / Crypto News / Google (GOOGL) Stock; Flat to Slightly Lower as $5.7B Fluidstack Deal Sparks AI Infrastructure Bets / Google (GOOGL) Stock; Flat to Slightly Lower as $5.7B Fluidstack Deal Sparks AI Infrastructure Bets Author: Coincentral Source: Coincentral 2026/04/16 23:44 4 min read Share LONG $0.001959 -3.63% For feedback or concerns regarding this content, please contact us at crypto.news@mexc.com TLDRs; Alphabet shares stayed mostly flat as investors digested massive AI infrastructure financing news. Record $5.7B junk bond highlights rising demand for AI-driven data center expansion globally. Fluidstack partnership and Google backstop deepen exposure to long-term compute infrastructure buildout. Markets remain cautious despite strong AI demand due to debt load and energy risk concerns. Alphabet Inc. (GOOGL) shares traded flat to slightly lower as markets reacted to a landmark $5.7 billion high-yield bond deal tied to Google-linked data center development in Indiana. The financing, led by Morgan Stanley, will support the construction of two large facilities in Sullivan County designed to meet surging demand for artificial intelligence computing power. Although the deal represents one of the largest debt raises ever for data center infrastructure, investors appeared cautious rather than euphoric. The stock\u2019s muted reaction reflects a broader tension in the market: strong enthusiasm for AI growth paired with rising concern about financing complexity, energy usage, and long-term returns. The facilities are expected to be leased to Fluidstack, a cloud computing startup that brokers large-scale compute capacity for AI companies. Google\u2019s indirect involvement through financial backstops has drawn attention, signaling deeper exposure to the infrastructure layer of the AI economy. Fluidstack\u2019s Expanding Role Fluidstack has increasingly positioned itself as a key intermediary in the global AI infrastructure race. Founded at Oxford University, the company aggregates large blocks of computing capacity and matches them with AI labs requiring high-performance compute resources. Alphabet Inc., GOOGL The Indiana project reflects a broader strategy in which Fluidstack acts as a bridge between capital-heavy infrastructure developers and fast-growing AI companies. Its growing list of partnerships includes major AI players and long-term leasing agreements that stretch over a decade in some cases. This structure is becoming more common: AI companies demand compute capacity, intermediaries secure long-term leases, and infrastructure operators build and manage the physical data centers. Operators such as former crypto-mining firms are increasingly repurposing their energy and site expertise to meet AI demand. New AI Financing Model Emerges The $5.7 billion bond issuance signals a shift in how AI infrastructure is funded. Rather than relying solely on big tech balance sheets, the sector is moving toward project-based financing supported"

English English Indonesian \u65e5\u672c\u8a9e IDR / USD: ---- As of: More Google-affiliated data centre seeks jumbo funding via junk bonds ZH DK Zetta Hannany, Dhika Priambodo 16 April 2026, 08:48 WIB ZH Zetta Hannany Reporter DK Dhika Priambodo Reporter \u00d7 WhatsApp Channel A Google-linked data centre is seeking USD 5.7 billion via junk bonds to expand AI infrastructure. WASHINGTON \u2013 A data centre affiliated with Alphabet Inc. through its Google unit is exploring a USD 5.7 billion fundraising through high-yield (junk) bond issuance. If realised, the transaction would mark the largest financing deal for large-scale artificial intelligence (AI) infrastructure expansion. As reported by Bloomberg , Morgan Stanley has begun marketing the bonds on Wednesday and is expected to price the deal in the near term, according to sources familiar with the matter. Proceeds from the issuance will be used to build two data centres in Sullivan County, Indiana, which will later be leased to cloud computing start-up Fluidstack Ltd. with support from Google. Read Also: US halts some chip equipment shipments to China\u2019s Hua Hong The rapid acceleration of AI development has led to significant shortages in data centre capacity, GPU chips and access to high-speed electricity. To finance these needs, companies are increasingly turning to various debt instruments, ranging from high-yield bonds to project financing. At USD 5.7 billion, the issuance would be the largest US dollar-denominated junk bond tied to data centre financing, testing investor appetite for the sector, while also marking the biggest transaction led by a single Wall Street institution, Morgan Stanley. The deal could also surpass a previous record, likewise led by Morgan Stanley, involving a high-yield bond issuance for crypto miner TeraWulf Inc., which is also backed by Google. The five-year bonds will be issued by Meridian Arc HoldCo LLC, a joint venture between Next Frontier LLC and Fluidstack. Fluidstack focuses on high-performance computing-based data centre development, a sector experiencing rapid growth alongside rising AI demand. Recently, Fluidstack also announced a USD 50 billion partnership with Anthropic to build data centres dedicated to large language model development. In addition, the company is expanding the use of data centre facilities operated by TeraWulf in New York, while Google has increased both its financing support and equity stake in TeraWulf to around 14%. PHOTO: REUTERS [NEW YORK] Data centres linked to Alphabet\u2019s Google are seeking to raise US$5.7 billion from a junk-bond sale, in what would be the largest deal of its kind to finance the artificial intelligence buildout boom. Morgan Stanley kicked off its marketing on Wednesday (Apr 15), and is expected to price the notes as soon as this week, said a source. The proceeds will fund the construction of two data centres on a campus in Sullivan County, Indiana, which will be leased to cloud-computer startup Fluidstack and backstopped by Google, the source added. The rapid expansion of AI has created an unprecedented shortage of data-centre space, graphics-processing unit chips and quick access to electricity to power it all. To fund all of that, companies are tapping every corner of the debt markets from junk bonds to project finance. At US$5.7 billion, the deal marks the largest US dollar junk bond to test investor demand for data-centre debt, and the biggest to be led by one Wall Street institution \u2013 Morgan Stanley \u2013 based on Bloomberg-compiled data. The new deal would mean that the bank beats its own prior record. It had a sole-led high-yield bond offering for cryptocurrency miner TeraWulf, which was a deal that was also backstopped by Google. Fluidstack helps develop data centres through building and operating high-performance computing infrastructure, an area of business that is booming amid the rise of AI. Former Husker volleyball coach has positive message for student-athletes Close Facebook Twitter WhatsApp SMS Email Print Copy article link Save Community Event at Town Square Las Vegas Will Bring Together Local Leaders, Startups and the Region's Growing AI \"/> TensorWave Celebrates Opening of Las Vegas Headquarters With Community Ribbon-Cutting and Block Party Apr 16, 2026 Apr 16, 2026 Comments Facebook Twitter WhatsApp SMS Email Facebook Twitter WhatsApp SMS Email Print Copy article link Save Community Event at Town Square Las Vegas Will Bring Together Local Leaders, Startups and the Region's Growing AI Ecosystem LAS VEGAS, NV / ACCESS Newswire / April 16, 2026 / TensorWave , a rapidly growing AI infrastructure company, today announced the opening of its new Las Vegas headquarters, marking a major milestone for the company and a continued investment in Nevada's expanding technology ecosystem. To commemorate the occasion, Commissioner Michael Naft recently visited TensorWave's headquarters and presented a proclamation recognizing the company's contributions to innovation, job creation, and the region's growing technology sector. The new headquarters reflects TensorWave's commitment to building world-class AI infrastructure while supporting the growing community of startups, entrepreneurs, and technology innovators in the region. As the company continues to expand, TensorWave expects to create new jobs throughout the year, further investing in local talent and the broader innovation economy. To celebrate this milestone, TensorWave will host a community AI Builders Block Party and Ribbon-Cutting on April 24, 2026, from 5-7 p.m. PT at Town Square Las Vegas, bringing together members of the local tech community, entrepreneurs, civic leaders, and industry partners. Following the celebration, TensorWave will partner with StartupVegas to host a startup pitch competition at the company's Las Vegas office from 7-8:30 p.m. PT, highlighting emerging founders and companies shaping the future of technology in Nevada. \"Las Vegas is quickly becoming a center for innovation and entrepreneurship,\" said Piotr Tomasik, co-founder, President and COO of TensorWave and co-founder of StartupVegas. \"Opening our headquarters here reflects our belief in the region's growing technology ecosystem and"

    "content": "NPS pieces together AI playbook - Needham Observer Close Search for: Search News Open dropdown menu Town Government Schools & Youth Boards & Briefs Community Police Blotter Needham Votes 2026 About Us Newsletters Open dropdown menu Weekly Newsletters Printable Recent Posts Calendar Open dropdown menu View Events Submit Event Contact Subscribe Donate Homepage Close Skip to content Needham Observer Free, Independent, Hyperlocal News about Needham, Massachusetts Open Search Search for: Search Donate Menu News Open dropdown menu Town Government Schools & Youth Boards & Briefs Community Police Blotter Needham Votes 2026 About Us Newsletters Open dropdown menu Weekly Newsletters Printable Recent Posts Calendar Open dropdown menu View Events Submit Event Contact Subscribe Donate Homepage Home \u00bb NPS pieces together AI playbook Posted in Tech NPS pieces together AI playbook by Kay Matipa April 29, 2026 April 29, 2026 Share this: Chris Gosselin leads \"AI in NPS\" presentation at the School Committee meeting on April 28./ Credit: Needham Observer IPads have bumped out boxy beige computers, Google Apps have digitalized group projects, and new forms of Artificial Intelligence have made a dramatic entrance by way of ChatGPT, Gemini and Claude. As modern technology continues to shape 21st-century schooling, Needham educators are building an offensive approach to AI\u2019s presence in the classroom. Their strategy rides on three things: clear guidelines, critical evaluation and AI literacy. A presentation to the school committee on April 28, led by Chris Gosselin, director of Instructional Technology and Innovation for Needham Public Schools, explored the district\u2019s implementation of Generative AI. While traditional AI analyzes existing data and makes predictions (i.e. Netflix recommendations), Generative AI creates new, original content by picking up patterns from existing data (i.e. ChatGPT). This kind of interactive AI is where the schools\u2019 focus currently lies. GenAI\u2019s public debut in 2022 did not come with an instruction manual, said Gosselin, leaving schools in a bit of a lurch. \u201c[It] was released pretty publicly to students and teachers and all members of our society, pretty much all at once,\u201d he said. \u201cIn education, we like a plan, and we didn\u2019t really get a plan. We didn\u2019t get a runway for AI \u2014 we got AI.\u201d Teachers and students found themselves in the same boat: exploring and responding to GenAI as it appeared. When AI first dropped, Gosselin noted, training teachers was the primary concern. \u201cWe felt that was where we had to start so our students could then be supported in using AI appropriately in instructional settings,\u201d he said. Although teachers may have received the first round of trainings, the district\u2019s multipronged AI strategy seeks to engage both staff and students in the responsible, proactive use of AI tools. \u201cThere is a pressure, sometimes, on teachers to be the expert,\u201d Gosselin said. \u201cAnd when we think about the ever-changing landscape of technology, there has to be a vulnerability, right? As an adult, especially working with a class of 24 high school students, you have to be vulnerable and say, \u2018You know what? These kids in"

Across industries, we\u2019re hearing a consistent message from enterprise organizations: they need to modernize core systems, operationalize AI, and run workloads across multiple clouds without added complexity. Many of them want hybrid flexibility, predictable operations, and trusted AI, but integrating platforms, models, and tooling across cloud providers remains a major challenge. This is where IBM\u2019s collaboration with Google Cloud can help to deliver real value. By bringing together AI capabilities from both companies, Google Cloud\u2019s strength in developer-centric design with IBM\u2019s strengths in hybrid cloud, data management, and automation, we can help clients build a more unified and flexible foundation for their AI and cloud strategies. This work is grounded in what clients have told us they need, which is interoperability, choice, and confidence that their systems will scale securely. Rather than forcing a single path, this creates a more open, secure, and flexible foundation for hybrid cloud and AI transformation. It also aims to make it easier for clients to connect data, models, infrastructure, and operations across environments. This is a clearer and more practical approach to modernizing systems and deploying AI at scale. Capabilities available today include: IBM watsonx.data is now available on Google Cloud Marketplace enabling enterprises to manage real-time, unstructured and multimodal data for AI at scale. HashiCorp Terraform Enterprise, Vault, and Consul are available in the Google Cloud Marketplace to help clients secure, automate, and orchestrate cloud services and workloads. Confluent Cloud , the fully managed Apache Kafka service, is available on Google Cloud Marketplace allowing customers to build next-gen data architectures with real-time, contextual data. Red Hat OpenShift , including Red Hat OpenShift Virtualization, is now accessible directly through the Google Cloud Console and available on Google Cloud Marketplace. For customers using both virtual machines (VMs) and containers, this simplifies onboarding, provides unified billing, and the ability for customers to apply their committed Google Cloud spend. Engineering teams have validated Red Hat OpenShift and Red Hat Enterprise Linux at Google Cloud scale, giving clients a stable hybrid foundation for modern workloads.     "content": "Together AI Moves Drastically Increases Headquarters Size Skip to content Skip to site index Sign in Subscribe Subscribe search Commercial San Francisco A Together AI leases 150K sf for new HQ in SF\u2019s Showplace Square Tech firm\u2019s hub size jumps nearly 5,000% on heels of AI boom RREEF Property Trust's Todd Henderson and Together AI's Vipul Ved Prakash with 2 Henry Adams Street, San Francisco (Together AI, Google Maps, RREEF Property Trust) Apr 20, 2026, 9:57 PM UTC By TRD Staff Save article SHARE THIS ARTICLE FONT SIZE A A A Together AI will soon move into a new 150,000-square-foot headquarters in San Francisco\u2019s Showplace Square. The neocloud start-up inked a deal for two floors of the roughly 329,000-square-foot office building at 2 Henry Adams Street, the San Francisco Business Times reported . Financial terms of the six-year lease from majority owner RREEF Property Trust were not disclosed. Broker JLL represented Together, while Cushman & Wakefield represented the landlords. Together plans to move into the 111-year-old brick building this summer from its current digs at 251 Rhode Island Street, a couple of blocks away, where it had initially rented 3,000 square feet three years ago. The move represents a 4,900 percent jump in office space for the fast growing tech firm, which provides services and infrastructure to allow companies to train and deploy generative AI models in the cloud. The company now employs just more than 300 people, Kai Mak, its chief revenue officer, told the Business Times. By the end of the year, it aims to employ at least 500 workers. \u201cAs we\u2019re in this hypergrowth phase with our business, we just want to be in a sustainable place that can have the envelope to fit our growth,\u201d he said. SIGN UP The startup joins a legion of aggressive startups with links to San Francisco\u2019s booming AI sector expanding at light speed across the city, such as Anthropic . Many of those companies, like Together, are adding to their offices in fits and starts \u2014 a departure from technology tenants of the past decade, which generally snapped up blocks of offices at once. The dynamic is helping drive the recovery of San Francisco\u2019s office market, which has seen its office vacancy fall to 30.8 percent in the first three months of the year, down from 32.8 percent at the end of last year, according to CBRE. The decline this quarter comes as San Francisco tenants absorbed 1.8 million square feet of offices \u2013 the highest quarterly net absorption since CBRE began tracking it. Even after a quarter\u2019s worth of robust leasing and blockbuster deals from Anthropic and OpenAI , demand for offices in the city has not declined, according to Colin Yasukochi, executive director of CBRE\u2019s Tech Insights Center. While AI firms are driving the leasing frenzy , they\u2019ve helped convince tenants in other sectors to close the vacancy gap. Mak said he soon expects to see \u201csome potential monster IPOs,\u201d which could usher in even more growth among"

    "content": "NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Nano Omni Launches on Together AI for Multimodal AI | MEXC News Buy Crypto Markets Spot Futures GOLD Earn Event Center More Together AI integrates NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Nano Omni, a multimodal AI model, offering developers scalable, efficient reasoning across video, audio, and text. (Read Together AI integrates NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Nano Omni, a multimodal AI model, offering developers scalable, efficient reasoning across video, audio, and text. (Read MEXC Exchange / Crypto News / NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Nano Omni Launches on Together AI for Multimodal AI / NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Nano Omni Launches on Together AI for Multimodal AI Author: BlockChain News Source: BlockChain News 2026/04/29 00:57 3 min read Share For feedback or concerns regarding this content, please contact us at crypto.news@mexc.com NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Nano Omni Launches on Together AI for Multimodal AI Zach Anderson Apr 28, 2026 16:57 Together AI integrates NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Nano Omni, a multimodal AI model, offering developers scalable, efficient reasoning across video, audio, and text. Together AI has announced the immediate availability of NVIDIA\u2019s Nemotron 3 Nano Omni model on its platform, marking a significant step forward for multimodal artificial intelligence. The Nemotron 3 Nano Omni is designed to unify reasoning across video, audio, images, and text in a single inference pass, offering developers a powerful tool for building complex, agentic applications at scale. The Nemotron 3 Nano Omni represents NVIDIA\u2019s latest foray into hybrid model architecture. The 30-billion parameter Mixture of Experts (MoE) design activates only 3 billion parameters per token during processing, leveraging multi-token prediction for efficient computation. According to Together AI, this architecture unlocks faster and more cost-effective multimodal reasoning while maintaining low latency\u2014a critical factor for real-world AI deployments. Why Together AI? Developers leveraging Together AI\u2019s infrastructure gain access to a fully managed environment optimized for production-scale workloads. The platform\u2019s tight integration with Nemotron 3 Nano Omni removes the operational complexity of managing GPUs, allowing teams to prototype and deploy AI applications more efficiently. Together AI also offers secure APIs for developers, ensuring data protection without sacrificing performance. One standout feature of Nemotron 3 Nano Omni is its ability to process up to 256,000 tokens of shared context across multiple input formats. This eliminates the need for fragmented pipelines\u2014common in multimodal AI systems where separate models handle vision, audio, and text inputs. By consolidating these tasks, the model reduces latency, prevents errors from compounding, and simplifies system architecture. Key Advantages Streamlined Multimodal Processing: Handles video, audio, and document reasoning in one model, reducing the need for multiple pipelines. Scalability: Highly efficient with support for NVIDIA Hopper and Blackwell architectures, offering flexible deployment options from the cloud to on-premises systems. Open Framework: Provides developers with open weights, data, and recipes, ensuring no lock-in and full data control. Applications in Focus The unified reasoning capabilities of Nemotron 3 Nano Omni unlock a wide range of use cases: Customer Service: AI agents can simultaneously interpret call recordings, screen captures, and policy documents, improving response accuracy and efficiency. (Atlanta News First) \u2014 Business, education and security leaders have gathered this week at Atlanta Tech Village in Buckhead for Atlanta AI Week. The event is designed to discuss how Atlanta can stay at the forefront of technological change and how people can adapt to it. \u201cIt might scare some folks,\u201d said Summer Crenshaw, who organized Atlanta AI Week. The event is expected to draw more than 500 organizations. \u201cWe need folks talking about the hard stuff, and coming together as a community,\u201d Crenshaw said. As AI expands into everything, businesses are looking to set roots in hub cities. Atlanta\u2019s ability to develop a cohesive plan for supporting AI businesses will go a long way toward bringing more money into the region. \u201cAtlanta is pretty advanced overall when we look at the nation as a whole,\u201d Crenshaw said, adding that more and more companies are establishing locations outside traditional hubs in Boston and Silicon Valley.
